The 171-line compiler has zero Game-member references. Game gains a cache handle and one composition method; its existing mutable actor dependencies are not claimed as removed. Other literal mentions are inherited/dynamic Node APIs or constants. Total runtime text grows by 131 lines: this improves a common editing path and eliminates repeated math rather than claiming a net code-size reduction. No second authoritative player, card or graft state is created; rollback restores the original facts and the next query reuses/recompiles their value.

CPU comparison: 240 fixed 60 Hz headless frames, first 60 warmup, four players (bow/arbalest/staff/fan), three refined affinity slots and two Common weapon cards each, no grafts, twelve ordinary high-HP enemies plus leader, seeded map/combat RNG and four ordinary launch attempts per frame. Twelve additional native-build queries per measured frame fall from **1.079 to 0.345 ms** (about 68%); physics CPU is **1.148 to 1.152 ms/frame**, presentation **2.149 to 2.027 ms/frame**, weapon stage **11 to 11 microseconds/frame**, and launch attempts **19 to 18 microseconds/frame**. Weapon sequences remain `[5,4,6,6]`. These are workload-local CPU observations, not FPS/GPU, snapshot encoding, or internet measurements.

Final focused evidence: **33 suites / 46 strict fixture-process verdicts**, including all fifteen weapons, five connected trees, nine families, actual card/graft consumers, runtime/display agreement, cache invalidation, received details, rollback, TC01, five independent host/guest pairs and two four-player controls with fifth-client refusal. Matching builds pass authoritative action readiness; definition mismatch is specifically refused with zero guest admissions; RPC-contract refusal is separate. The Oracle pair's uncontrolled committed attack could spend the Ward during its observation wait; its fixture now holds both committed/new enemy attacks until the explicit incoming-hit checks, preserving all block/recovery assertions. All **106 validation-tool self-tests** pass.

The retained `garden_spring` fixture separately fails its older research-only spring-reveal setup before its Core checks: current `GardenInteractionService.allowed` requires the physical constructed spring and actual landmark positions. No Garden gameplay or acceptance assertion is weakened here; the user has confirmed that the dedicated spring will be replaced by Garden water features. Retire this obsolete fixture alongside that replacement and cover the agreed water interactions; do not rebuild its old reveal contract. The replacement is not implemented in this refactor. The earlier `guarded_expedition` combined-settlement assertion also remains outside this boundary. Neither is counted as passing evidence. Production AC-06 activation remains off and host-crash loss of uncommitted progression remains a known limitation.

Registry **228 → 229** adds the owner unit. Six obsolete source overlays are retired, two retained anchors rerouted and one trimmed only to remove replaced dodge calculation text. The conservative shared-contract plan touches **229/229** suites; only the selected interaction checks are refreshed. Broad release evidence remains deferred. Protocol 41, RPC signatures, snapshot layout, authored gameplay definitions and save configuration are unchanged. All test data roots are disposable; main, protected tags, stashed references and real saves are preserved. No export, push, manual visual acceptance or separate-PC/internet verification is claimed. Remaining work is the planned replication/detail projection boundary and narrower specialist/Garden world adapters, not another gameplay ruleset.

## Host publication and detached state projections

Starting point: `719ca595b2b19ba40c69f3fe53cff003cc3257eb`, protected by `architecture/pre-world-publication-20261119`. [`WorldReplication`](../world_replication.gd) now owns the outgoing host publication sequence instead of Game and NetworkPresentation sharing mutable send state. It receives a focused port, never Game, an actor dictionary or an ENet peer. It is a per-world RefCounted owner, not a new global manager or second gameplay state store.

| Interface / editing location | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `publish(source, delta_seconds, force)` | Observe existing reliable health, select eligible peers, advance the unchanged 20 Hz fast / 4 Hz details clocks, encode one shared fast replacement and deliver recipient-specific envelopes. Forced committed-cleanup publication uses this same path. A late tick produces at most one replacement per channel, preserving the previous phase/debt rule. |
| `sequence()`, `clear_session()` | Detached outgoing sequence inspection and one authoritative teardown. Clear retires the frame/key/sequence while preserving the existing cadence phase. Join, leave and Arena join clear this owner alongside the existing receive/prediction owners. Session invalidation stops an in-progress old batch; a recursive callback cannot start another publication. |
| `world_replication_source.gd` | The sole world adapter for this host path. `_public_player` reads health/life/cooldown/protection owners plus existing actor movement/loadout facts. `owner_details` reads only the selected recipient's build, cards, grafts, inventory and support presentation. No public method returns a mutable actor/profile, ledger, owner service or transport peer. |
| Shared and private capture | Capture the public roster once per fast frame. Capture common combat events/warnings/deaths once per details burst and private data once per intended owner. Fast recipient flags are separate from reliable `owner_binding`, so private action-stamp calculation is not promoted to 20 Hz. Garden and roster data remain shared; inventory, connection stamps and optional recovery remain recipient-specific. |
| Recovery / transport | Only a successfully encoded bounded frame advances the sequence/cache. Throttled reliable details reuse the latest same-floor frame, with viewer flags applied to a copy. Invalid occupancy is refused, never truncated. Existing Source transport methods invoke the unchanged Game RPCs; the codec, channel numbers and receiving order remain unchanged. |

The two Game bodies become integration/query facades, replacing 103 lines of embedded assembly/execution with two calls. Five mutable transport fields move: Game's two clocks and NetworkPresentation's outgoing sequence/latest-frame/key. No duplicate copies remain. At that checkpoint, NetworkPresentation still retained reliable-health revisions, incoming sequence/freshness and roster presentation. The next section completes guest receive/reconciliation. Host reliable-health publication is consolidated in the completion below. Game RPC entry points and remaining actor/world integration remain dependencies.

The new owner has **105** lines and zero Game-member references. The projection port has **165** lines and **52** literal world-member sites: **11** other Game-field reads, **18** owner/service accesses, **19** Game operations, one Game constant and three Node/dynamic accesses. These are relocated integration reads; moving them into a port is not elimination of whole-project coupling. The owner controls real transport state and execution, and outside callers receive detached values.

| Consistent source measurement | Before | After |
|---|---:|---:|
| Game physical lines / functions / plain and onready variables | 3,716 / 247 / 103 | 3,615 / 247 / 103 |
| NetworkPresentation variables | 12 | 9 |
| Public-roster captures in a forced four-player fast+details burst | 7 | 1 |
| Common event/warning/death captures per three-recipient details burst | 3 | 1 |
| Tracked runtime GDScript lines, excluding tests/tools | 59,433 | 59,598 |

Two new Game handles replace the two raw clocks; existing query and publication entry points remain because live callers use them. The function count is unchanged; 101 central lines are removed. Runtime text grows with explicit detached records and the small port; no net project-code deletion is claimed. Transport publication state is not rolled back with gameplay transactions: a sent sequence must never be reused. Existing guarded publication gates and committed/aborted/uncertain decisions remain authoritative.

The uncertainty fixture exposed an existing delayed admission-rejection callback capturing a freed Game. `SessionWorld.reject` now captures a WeakRef and refuses a missing, detached or deleting world before its unchanged transport/binding checks. This is a bounded network lifetime fix; it does not change reward settlement or activate production persistence.

CPU sample: 150 measured forced-publication assembly bundles after 25 warmup; four class players, three refined affinity slots each, twenty scouts plus leader; one fast frame and three compressed owner-details/recovery envelopes. Median assembly CPU is **3.577 → 2.813 ms** (about **21%** less). This excludes transport, physics, rendering and GPU work. Reliable compressed sizes vary with contents/order; the full fast frame stays **1,196 bytes** under the unchanged 1,200-byte application budget. No full-frame/FPS or internet-performance claim is made.

Final evidence passes **21 current source suites / 44 strict process verdicts**, plus two five-process guarded-disconnect variants (**54 total fixture-process passes**). The cleanup-pending variant sends exactly one final replacement; the uncertainty variant sends none and cannot replay settlement/readmission. All **106 validation-tool self-tests** pass. The retained Arena class compiles with the new inherited publication interfaces; this is not a fresh Arena gameplay/training campaign.

Coverage includes payload/codec round-trip, independent health and state freshness, deterministic cadence/refusal/reentry/session invalidation, detached private inspections, controlled missed-fast recovery, actual cards/grafts, movement, generated-Garden return/rejoin, guarded travel/rollback, voluntary forfeiture, four standard four-player controls and their fifth-client refusals. Matching builds pass authoritative action readiness. Bad-definition admission is specifically refused with zero authenticated guests; RPC-contract coverage is separate. In the two instrumented mixed-affinity parties, all three guests observe eight markers: maximum intervals between applied states range **76.50–86.78 ms**; maximum marker-observation waits range **52.02–59.05 ms**. These are separate measurements. Full occupancy reaches 1,196 bytes on the real local connections. Controlled lost/reordered/duplicate/older recovery frames use the production receiver and cannot replay effects or restore a departed wallet. Local loopback does not establish separate-PC or internet acceptance.

Two pre-existing codec-fixture assumptions are corrected without changing their assertions: copied Wilds actors lose a Garden-only revision, and the reliable recovery envelope includes its required current Garden layout. Earlier type-annotation failures and the first movement-history/root-border error runs are retained. The original movement assertions pass on independent recheck and final verification; no acceptance bound is relaxed. The first Garden rejoin run's error came from unchanged `menu_button_roots.gd`; both assertions and strict engine-error checks pass on recheck/final. The `expedition_pressure_coop` warning/spawn setup still fails with both current and exact pre-refactor publication methods in disposable controls. It remains registered, unchanged in its assertions, and is not counted as passing. No reinforcement behavior is changed. The earlier guarded_expedition combined-settlement assertion and the separately planned spring/water replacement remain outside this slice.

The registry grows **229 → 230**, adding `world_replication`. Twelve superseded Game source overlays retire; no supported suite is deleted. The shared declaration/adapter change conservatively invalidates **230/230** source keys. Only focused evidence is refreshed; broad source/package release refresh remains separately scheduled. Protocol 41, all 25 Game RPC signatures/NodePaths, gameplay tuning, the codec and save configuration remain unchanged. Production AC-06 activation remains off. Real saves, main, protected tags and six stashed design references are untouched; there is no export, push or main promotion.

## Guest receive and reconciliation ownership

Starting point: `5455150a47dbdc5d5a94f1d67907d7622194093f`, protected by `architecture/pre-guest-replication-20261119`. [`GuestReplication`](../guest_replication.gd) owns accepted incoming state. It receives a focused projection port and decoded/wire values, never Game or mutable gameplay actors. Receiving state cannot cast, grant rewards, debit a wallet or replay combat.

| Public operation / editing location | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `receive_frame(world, bytes, recovery)` | Decode through the unchanged codec; reject stale sequences; reconcile stronger reliable health, names and owner-only details; deliver one accepted projection. Duplicate/older recovery cannot undo a newer fast state. Existing observation timings remain separate from application timings. |
| `receive_details(world, payload, garden_bytes, inventory, key)` | Bound decompression; validate the current connection and required terrain envelope; accept detached private details/inventory/progress and receive time; apply optional recovery through the same frame path. Generated Garden layout goes to the terrain owner and is not retained as a duplicate receiver cache. |
| `receive_vitals`, `receive_event`, `events` | Reconcile health revisions and one-time damage flashes; keep the existing newest-100 transient-event limit, floor filter and expiry tail. `events` adds the current owner-detail events without issuing gameplay actions. |
| `details`, `garden_progress`, `inventory`, `scouts`, `campaign`, scalar diagnostics | Detached inspection only. Garden progress contains fields 0–16, not the generated layout. Bulk scout readers take one local copy; scalar enemy/campaign queries avoid whole-buffer copies. The former `core_details_at` metadata is an explicit private receive timestamp. |
| `clear_session` | Retire details, health revisions, names, events, inventory, world display buffers and freshness counters together. Normal session and Arena joins/leaves use the same operation. |
| [`guest_replication_world.gd`](../guest_replication_world.gd) | Apply accepted facts to existing participation, tactics, terrain and prediction owners; install the current `Game.players` display projection. It stores no received buffers and returns no actor/service dictionaries. Game retains its unchanged RPC declarations and five thin receive/event integration methods. |

Twenty scattered fields transfer: eleven Game buffers/flags, eight NetworkPresentation receive fields and one metadata timestamp. The old fields, guest NetView methods and raw mutation paths are removed. There is no second guest actor roster or host HP authority. `Game.players` remains the current display/prediction projection; host actor admission/movement/loadout remains coupled. The host-health completion below removes the remaining separate publication service.

## Host health publication completion

The completion from `3b8ece4d7f26a3b8c9fcf987a656be08562192a1`, protected by `architecture/pre-host-health-publication-20261119`, folds the last host publication state into the existing WorldReplication owner. |---|---|

| Diagnostics | `Journal.player_state` accepts an optional scalar revision. Incoming-hit, class-request and host diagnostic callers query the publisher; guest diagnostics retain their accepted wire revision. Health/damage tuning stays in its existing content and combat owners. |

## Ordered world presentation

From `b4c9c1d854761e925b61fe9e2c1e1e78bbe0fff3`, protected by `architecture/pre-world-presentation-20261119`, [`WorldPresentation`](../world_presentation.gd) coordinates one explicit presentation pipeline. Its constructor receives Stage, death/combat views, the exploration map and the two audio nodes. Public frame operations receive detached display facts, never Game, actor records, profiles or gameplay owners. This is presentation responsibility transfer, not a new gameplay-state owner.

| Operation / editing location | Contract |
|---|---|
| `prepare(warnings)` | Prepare Glasscap presentation and attack-pose metadata before actor drawing. Game still owns avatar/terrain drawing and scoped fact capture. |
| `present_effects(frame)` | After drawing: death effects → real-clock EventAudio → combat geometry → map → sight/Glasscap sync. Preserve floor/participation keys, current scene geometry and the originally captured gate point. Garden audio still plays while combat geometry is suppressed. |
| `present_garden(delta, frame)` | Preserve plots → Heart → sound mix → Garden order. The mix sees freshly displayed research. Game reuses one existing Heart snapshot instead of three reads; research progression stays with HeartProgress. |
| `map_expanded()`, `sight_radius()` | Scalar inspection only. Inventory-drop visibility no longer accesses Game's raw map node. Underlying map/range rules stay unchanged. |

Three Game fields (`death_visuals`, `combat_visuals`, `exploration`) retire in favor of one controller reference. Their Nodes are still constructed once at the composition root, with the original parents and attachment order. The controller has six private presentation-node references and **zero Game-member sites**. Existing renderers retain event receipts, lifetimes and caches; no second set of components or authoritative buffers is created. Twenty-six fixtures now locate named view Nodes through the fixture-only `tests/support/world_presentation_case.gd`; production code exposes no component getter.

These are not claims of project-code deletion or elimination of Stage/component dependencies. The benefit is one inspectable frame order and a standalone interface that can be tested without Game. Authorization, network/RPC entry points, avatar drawing, spectator targeting, HUD fact capture and Garden domain commands remain with their existing integrations.

Neither assertion is changed or counted as passing. The previous portal/spring/shrine, reinforcement-setup and combined-settlement fixture limitations remain separate; the planned spring-to-Garden-water replacement is not implemented here.

CPU: 150 synchronous presentation updates after 20 warmup, generated Garden, four initialized players and no active combat. Protocol 41, all 25 RPC declarations/NodePaths, codec, 20 Hz publication and gameplay values are unchanged. The full-occupancy fixture and the live CR04C four-player trace reach the same **1,196-byte** fast application payload; CR04B's live trace peaks at 1,091 bytes with fifteen scouts. ## Garden view ports

From `7ec0c9f6349017ce98b9da0b209f841d10e8d0b8`, protected by `architecture/pre-garden-view-ports-20261119`, six Garden views no longer receive Game or mutable actors. They configure explicit Stage/shell/presentation references, accept detached display frames, and emit refresh/request signals. Their existing visual Nodes, ordering, overlay ownership, cue baselines, crop-cycle requests and pending-click limits remain intact. The old Game-taking `mount`, `flower_shortcut` and actor-taking prompt interfaces retire; there is one current rendering path.

| Editing location | Responsibility / interface |
|---|---|
| `garden_interaction_view.gd` | Compose the existing six views and growth playback; observation mesh, journal, prompt priority and post-commit pulse. `present_feature` routes display frames; `refresh_requested` / `requested` do not authorize actions. |
| `garden_crop_view.gd`, `garden_care_panel.gd` | Crop inspector, quick watering and flower intentions; station choices, inventory availability and feedback. Content names come directly from canonical seed/part/campaign catalogs. Incoming frames are copied; no profile or inventory is edited. |
| `garden_return_view.gd`, `garden_terrace_view.gd`, `garden_ambience.gd` | Committed-discovery traces, conduit display and bounded cosmetic life. Stage/scenes are explicit presentation dependencies; no parent Game/service lookup remains. |
| `garden_presentation_world.gd` | One 118-line integration port with a weak world reference. `frame(kind, selection)` supplies scoped values; `request` accepts only Garden-action and research-audio intentions. `bind` connects the views. `interface_layers` separately supplies presentation Nodes. A removed world yields empty data and accepts no request. This adapter still has 43 world-member sites; those are remaining integration dependencies. |
| `garden_interaction_service.gd` | One `_observation_data` selector serves both the unchanged full network projection and `observation_projection`. `projection_epoch` avoids building the entire Garden tree for a scalar context. `garden_cultivation_world.gd::water_carried` similarly reads water status without constructing every crop. Mutating handlers, costs, rewards and persistence rules are unchanged. |

| Consistent source measurement | Before | After |
|---|---:|---:|
| Game lines / functions / plain and onready variables | 3,461 / 247 / 91 | 3,464 / 247 / 92 |
| Direct Game-member sites in the six views | 142 | 0 |
| Game references stored by those views | 6 | 0 |
| Total text in the six views | 606 | 655 |
| Runtime GDScript lines, excluding tests/tools | 59,793 | 59,985 |

Game gains three composition lines and one integration handle. Total runtime grows by 192 lines for the explicit interfaces and narrow queries. This is a complete view-boundary improvement, not central-line deletion or elimination of all Game coupling. The port owns no Garden, actor, wallet, crop or receipt state. Views retain only nonauthoritative display/playback data; existing domain owners remain authoritative. New artwork or text can be changed in the named view without tracing actor/profile access.

The first draft measured 2.082 ms versus a 1.723 ms baseline. Narrow observation/epoch/water queries removed redundant complete projections and copying. Final median is **1.723 → 1.597 ms** for 300 synchronous world-and-six-view updates after 40 warmup, generated Garden, four initialized players and three planted starter beds. This headless CPU workload includes scene/UI/audio/map/Garden presentation and excludes physics, sockets and GPU/render-driver work. It is not an FPS result. There is no new per-frame authoritative cache or invalidation framework.

Final verification passes **24 current-input suites / 39 strict process verdicts**: fifteen units, seven independent host/guest pairs and two four-player controls with fifth-client refusal. The standalone test installs no Game and verifies all six views, detached data, cycle/revision requests, one pending click, context cleanup and cosmetic-only effects. The new co-op fixture uses the existing disposable guarded title route, actual researched/placed Offering Altar, real panel requests, one observation receipt, one crop choice, one trophy debit, Garden return/rejoin and detached guest inspection. Read-model parity is checked on host and guest. Host release/readmission is explicitly acknowledged; this is Garden rejoin, not mid-run crash recovery. Existing class/card/graft, terrain, growth, research, damage/revival, guarded rollback and compatibility controls pass. Matching builds pass authoritative action readiness; definitions mismatch is specifically refused with zero admitted guests. All **106 validation-tool self-tests** pass; sixteen touched/caller/Arena scripts compile. Compilation does not verify Arena gameplay.

Five older Garden fixtures failed before the refactor and reproduced the same errors at the initial interface cutover: `garden_interactions`, `garden_conduit`, `garden_spring`, `garden_cleanup_coop`, `garden_crop_care_coop`. They assume unplaced researched features, automatic fourth-bed creation or an unbuilt spring, and the observation fixture binds a second cinematic to one shell. Their failed results remain recorded and registered, excluded from the 24 passes. Those comparison runs precede the final projection-only optimization; they are not relabeled current passing evidence. The planned spring-to-Garden-water replacement remains separate.

The registry grows **233 → 235**. Twenty-eight superseded view/full-projection normalization overlays retire; one surviving flower-input anchor is narrowly rerouted. Mutation/transaction scopes remain intact. The conservative plan selects 235 suites; only focused evidence is refreshed, with exact-input reuse where valid. Broad source/package release refresh remains separately scheduled. All 25 RPC declarations/NodePaths, protocol-41 layout, balance, catalogs, save schema and production configuration are unchanged. Every engine root is disposable under `.godot/vd`. Production AC-06 stays off; uncommitted host-memory rewards and deferred crash/mid-run recovery remain known limitations. This is local automated loopback, not manual visual/listening or separate-PC/internet verification. Main, protected saves/tags and six stashed design references remain untouched; no export or push.

Next meaningful presentation work is actor/spectator integration, alongside the separately identified specialist combat and Game-bound domain services. These should be coherent boundaries, not another sequence of wrappers or small reference-count reductions. Static scene authoring and the Garden water replacement require their own task scope.

## Garden cultivation rules and execution

This slice starts at `eb5e6c93dc91960e2ee501bc5e802fd0b0e82d33`, protected by `architecture/pre-garden-cultivation-20261119`. Planting, care, watering and native harvest have one current execution path. No bed, crop timer, seed balance, profile or transaction state is duplicated.

| Editing location | Authoritative responsibility / contract |
|---|---|
| `garden_cultivation.gd` | Pure decisions and numerical rules over detached bed/recipient facts. `care_request` determines reject/no-op/focus/water/care/harvest outcomes, preserving cycle/revision ordering. `harvest_yield`, `familiarity_after`, `rare_harvest_chance`, `planted_bed` and `bed_projection` are the current rules. No Game, actor, owner, save operation or RNG enters this module. Native seed/part amounts and the existing rare/familiarity constants have one clearly named home here. |
| `garden_cultivation_world.gd` | One weak world reference; no authoritative gameplay fields. Public operations are `plant_seed`, `harvest`, `harvest_seeds`, `command`, `eligible`, `focus`, `fill_water`, `spring_ready`, `water_carried`, `projection` and rejection feedback. It admits host requests, reads existing owners, passes detached bed facts into Rules, applies outcomes and retains actual transaction/publication/audio/diagnostic ordering. Guest projection queries now return detached data. Private harvest helpers are not alternate gameplay paths. |
| Existing owners / tuning | `progress.gd` still owns beds, growth work, Garden materials and save serialization. `cultivation_growth.gd` owns rates/migration; `seed_catalog.gd` owns species growing seconds; `garden_crop_catalog.gd` owns bud delay/rare modifiers; `garden_crop_state.gd` owns crop identities and frozen care rolls. Character/seed-reserve owners retain familiarity and stock. Transaction verdicts still come from `game_transaction.gd` and the persistence owners. |
| Game and interaction/presentation callers | Game wires `cultivation` and delegates authorized requests. Garden interaction dispatch and the presentation port call named peer/bed operations. No current caller passes Game or a mutable actor into a cultivation public method. RPC entry points, actor-profile scope and commit publication remain integration responsibilities. |

| Consistent source measurement | Before | After |
|---|---:|---:|
| Game lines / functions / plain and onready variables | 3,466 / 247 / 92 | 3,373 / 241 / 93 |
| Selected Game planting/harvest functions | 6 | 0 |
| Broad static `garden_crop_service.gd` implementation | 117 lines / 60 direct Game-member sites | Retired, including its UID and all active callers |
| New pure rules / Game-member sites | — | 88 lines / 0 |
| Runtime GDScript lines, excluding tests/tools | 60,084 | 60,190 |

The 229-line integration port retains **98 world-member sites**, including reads, state application and calls. Those integrate the former service and implicit Game-field accesses; they are remaining coupling, not 60 eliminated dependencies. Six real Game implementations retire with no Game forwarding wrappers. Overall runtime grows by 106 lines for the pure, independently testable rule boundary and explicit integration. Beds and rewards have no second authoritative cache. A harvest amount/chance change starts in the named Rules constants rather than Game; persistence and access checks stay visibly separate.

Existing values are unchanged: native harvest gives one ordinary part with its normal/refined roll or two seeds; familiarity gains one plus the existing Nursery benefit, capped at 9,999. The normal rare chance remains 5%, +4 percentage points for the first five familiarity levels, then +0.5 percentage points, capped at 45%; bud policies retain their own caps. Care previews freeze a copy of gameplay RNG without advancing it. A parts harvest still spends one ordinary draw even with a preserved roll; a seed harvest spends none. Save failure retains existing rollback/uncertainty distinctions and withholds success/audio as before. Growth rates, spring availability, water consumption, floor rewards, Class XP, cards and grafts are unchanged. The planned spring-to-Garden-water replacement is separate.

Pure tests cover all 24 species, exact seed/part/rare outcomes, familiarity bounds, frozen rolls, duplicate/stale requests, watering and concealed previews. Real checks cover care, seed progression, native harvest, guarded serializer failures/rollback/uncertain outcomes, growth, card/graft/class consumers and TC01. The existing real-title Garden pair additionally plants and harvests for the guest, confirms its canonical profile and detached direct queries, rejects duplicate harvest, and completes Garden rejoin without replay. Matching builds pass authoritative action readiness; mismatched definitions are specifically refused with zero admitted guests. They are excluded from the 22 passes. The conservative plan selects all 237 suites after the shared Game declaration change; only the bounded set is refreshed, with exact-key/intact-log reuse. Broad release evidence remains separately scheduled. This excludes physics, sockets and GPU work; no meaningful performance change or FPS result is claimed.

## Knowledge-unlocked Garden water

The pre-HG03 policy (2026-11-19) superseded the historical spring-construction/replacement notes above. The dedicated dormant/awake spring, watering-basin construction entry, placement exception and fixture shader are retired. Root Irrigation, with its existing Reaching Roots prerequisite, enables filling one watering vessel at a reachable dry bank of the water already present in the Garden. No second construction, material or Knowledge payment is required. Approach the water and press E, then water a growing crop. The existing once-per-crop 1.25× growth benefit, vessel consumption, transaction rollback and all research costs/times remain unchanged.

| Editing location | Current responsibility |
|---|---|
| `garden_water.gd` | Deterministic source and reachable-bank queries over generated terrain water only. The former two decorative ponds are removed. `nearby` validates the current actor position, water footprint, dry support and unobstructed approach on every request. `available`, `center` and `anchor` supply detached display facts. It has no Game, player, progression or transaction state. |
| `experimental/experiment_garden.gd` under `assets/terrain_m02/` | Owns one `water_sources` query instance per generated Garden. Its two private fields cache only the derived bank hint, keyed by current layout/research state and revalidated against current collision. This hint never authorizes filling. |
| `assets/terrain_m02/terrain_natural.gdshader` | Draws the actual generated water, including the existing discovery resonance. The independent pond generators, shoreline cache and mesh builders are retired. Terrain movement, bridges and projectiles retain their existing authority. |
| `garden_interaction_catalog.gd`, `heart_catalog.gd` | Existing `clearing_1` + `irrigation` prerequisites and canonical Knowledge description. The Knowledge preview reads the same description. Historical `sheltered_spring`, `spring_water`, `sheltered_water` and relationship IDs remain data/command compatibility, not an alternate fixture implementation. |
| `garden_cultivation_world.gd` / `garden_interaction_service.gd` | Existing host-authorized fill, observation and crop operations call the fresh source query. Progress remains the saved crop/research/observation owner. A fill changes only the existing one-vessel actor state and post-success cue; it grants no XP, items, research or random roll. |
| `garden_presentation_world.gd`, growth/return/ambience views and `soundscape.gd` | Show water interaction, unlock reveal, existing bounded moths and positional water ambience at the real water. The detached ambience frame contains only the bank position; views do not decide fill eligibility. |

Focused verification passes **27 current-input suites / 43 strict fixture-process verdicts**, including eight independent host/guest pairs and two four-player controls with fifth-client refusal. Checks cover Knowledge completion without construction, no fixture nodes/build option, real bank prompts, one vessel, duplicate/remote/blocked/submerged refusal, unchanged watering, saved observation identity, guest watering/rejoin, guarded rollback/uncertainty/cleanup outcomes, terrain/TC01, growth/reveals, audio/view integration, cards and travel. Matching clients pass authoritative action readiness; mismatched definitions receive the exact refusal with zero guest admissions. All **106 validation-tool self-tests** pass; **36 changed/caller/Arena scripts** compile. Compilation is not Arena gameplay verification. Earlier failed attempts remain recorded; the old spring assumptions in `garden_spring`, `cultivation_growth` and `garden_crop_care_coop` now have supported current replacements and pass. The unrelated recorded `foundations` refined-weapon assertion is not repaired or retested here.

Median headless CPU is **2.285 ms** over 300 synchronous world-and-six-Garden-view updates after 40 warmup, four players, three planted beds and Root Irrigation unlocked. This excludes physics, sockets and GPU work and is not FPS or a comparison to the earlier locked-water workload. Caching the derived hint avoids repeating the complete shoreline search for display; actual fill validation remains fresh.

The registry remains **237**; fifteen superseded position/mesh/source overlays retire without deleting a supported suite. Shared terrain/wiring inputs conservatively invalidate all 237 source keys; only this focused set is refreshed, with exact-input/intact-log reuse. Broad release evidence remains separately scheduled. The terrain compatibility signature is deliberately `modern-terrain-4-water-banks`; protocol-41 packet layout, RPCs and NodePaths are unchanged. Every peer must use the same updated build. Disposable fixtures only; production AC-06 remains disabled, host-crash/mid-run recovery remains deferred. Main, protected tags, real saves and the six stashed design references are preserved. No export, manual visual/listening acceptance or separate-PC/internet multiplayer is claimed.

## State ownership

| State | Current owner and writers | Readers | Intended boundary and migration risk |
|---|---|---|---|
| Progression and research view models | The progression/story views own detached presentation only; Game gathers facts; HeartProgress owns offering planning and existing research state. | Seed/class/Heart/story controls and their explicit refresh/action signals | Garden views now use detached ports. Keep progression, Altar, inventory and save authorities outside presentation. |
| Player-build view models | Inventory, loadout and summary own detached display state; Game gathers narrow owner facts; `player_build_projection.gd` formats pure summaries. | Three views, shell signals and relevant source/guest fixtures | Equipment, profiles, cards, grafts and wallet authority remain unchanged. Finish the remaining Garden adapters; do not move mutable actor records into a UI context. |
| Garden growth playback | `garden_growth_view.gd` owns private event/timer/context/retry and visibility restoration; its event inspection is detached. `GameplayUI` holds only a view reference. | Input/aim/developer eligibility and return-discovery presentation | The world adapter supplies scoped viewer facts; acknowledgements remain in `garden_growth_service.gd`. Keep Stage-only camera/formation metadata separate from gameplay authority. |
| Base world guidance | `WorldGuidance.project` is stateless presentation policy over detached scalar/content facts. No Game, mutable actor, wallet or profile enters it. | HUD context/session labels; standalone warning/priority checks and integrated co-op | Game gathers world facts and draws actors; WorldPresentation coordinates effects/audio/exploration. Source facts do not authorize actions. Crop/care/station overrides and their transactions stay with their existing owners. |
| World presentation pipeline | `WorldPresentation` holds six explicitly supplied presentation-node references and coordinates their existing operations. It owns no actor, profile, gameplay state or duplicate event cache. | Detached frame facts from Game; scalar map visibility/sight queries | Death/combat renderers, EventAudio, Soundscape and Exploration retain their receipts/caches. Stage still owns generated scenery. Avatar/spectator drawing receives detached facts; Garden domain integration remains separate. |
| Gameplay HUD and overlays | `GameplayUI` owns controls, exclusive frame visibility, confirmations and the displayed offer revision; authoritative cards/wallet/votes remain with their owners. | Input eligibility, detached HUD/menu projections, mounted feature views | The controller has no Game or actor input. Complete feature-panel read/command ports and context projection before declaring all UI isolated. |
| Avatar, spectator and dropped-item display | `actor_presentation_world.gd` gathers detached facts; Stage, SpectatorView and InventoryDrops own only render state. | Game frame wiring, shell refresh signals and Arena panel visibility | No Game/player input enters the views. The port has only a weak world reference; actor, health, lifecycle, participation and inventory owners remain authoritative. |
| Garden water access | `garden_water.gd` owns deterministic source/bank queries and one derived hint per generated Garden. `Progress` retains research and observation data; cultivation retains the one-vessel operation. | Host interactions and detached Garden presentation | No replacement basin or duplicated water currency. Fill authorization always queries current collision. |
| Garden crop commands and native harvest | `GardenCultivation` owns pure rules; `GardenCultivationWorld` integrates peer/bed operations without owning another crop store. Progress retains plots/materials; profiles and seed reserves retain familiarity/stock. | Garden action dispatch, crop/presentation queries and current transaction fixtures | No Game/actor crosses the rule API. Observation, conduit, return/discovery and persistence/session coordination remain separate. |
| Player registry, position and loadout | Host `Game.players` owns admitted actor records, position and loadout. Actors hold a read-only participation Link; no mutable `wild` field remains on the host. Accepted guest snapshots bind links to the receiving participation projection. | Combat, targeting, inventory, UI and snapshot builders | Actor admission, movement and equipment remain shared orchestration. Keep membership in ExpeditionParticipation and preserve character identity, disconnect settlement and guest prediction. |
| Player health | Each host actor holds an immutable `Health` value from `player_vitals.gd`. Damage, healing, loadout changes, revival and Vital grants use one value. | Combat and life systems, player snapshots, HUD, spectator and diagnostics | Project the effective capacity for guests. Keep damage counters, network revisions and the lost-HP penalty in their existing actor fields. Preserve rollback by replacing the value. |
| Player lifecycle | Each host actor holds an immutable `Life` value from `player_lifecycle.gd`. Game and expedition life replace it on a life transition. | Action admission, delayed combat and defense, movement, HUD and snapshots | Use the host generation for delayed work and keep the projected fallen flag on the existing wire. Preserve rollback through the actor savepoint. |
| Resolved outgoing contacts | `OutgoingHits` owns calculation/order; `OutgoingHitContext` binds one source and the current roster. Existing HP, tactics, reward and status owners retain their state. | Weapons, native class batches, traps and explicit secondary damage | No mutable actor crosses the execution API. WeaponAttackQueue, the separate Core/class delivery queues, learning counters and death/reward integration remain separate dependencies. |
| Weapon action timeline | `player_weapon_actions.gd::State` owns basic recovery, action sequence, committed direction, ranged wind-up and pose fields. Host actors hold one immutable value; WeaponAttackWorld, CoreCombat, class integration and lifecycle operations install successors. | Weapon admission/release, Stage, and existing pose/attack_anim snapshots | Keep held cursor input, queued projectiles, class cooldowns and damage resolution separate. Guests receive projections, not the host component. Fall/return clears active presentation without resetting the existing recovery or sequence. |
| Native player protection | `player_protection.gd::State` owns full-hit Guard/Ward duration, charges, directional and counter windows, counter power and accepted Ward claims. Native effects install immutable successors; IncomingPlayerHit consumes one block. | Native Q/R support, class follow-ups, incoming defense, life, status and motion projections | Captured CombatActionEvents retain their response budget and potency. HP, Bark, partial damage reduction, Slow and hit grace remain separate. The owner accepts scalar context, never Game or an actor dictionary. |
| Ordinary hit conditions | `player_hit_conditions.gd::State` owns ordinary Slow and post-hit/revival grace deadlines. IncomingPlayerHit, native cleanses and lifecycle operations install immutable successors. | Damage admission, Slow prevention, five-class support, Water, movement, status and HUD projections | Omens still own Quiet Ground grants/consumption. HP, Guard/Ward, Bark, other affinity statuses and cast queues remain separate. Guests receive existing projections, not this host component. |
| Resolved combat values | `ResolvedCombatBuild.Snapshot` freezes derived facts; its eight-entry cache owns only reproducible calculations, not another equipment/profile/card/graft authority. | Class/ordinary casts, Core selection, HUD, loadout, Tab, offers and graft quotes | Explicit class/loadout facts and immutable ledger references determine reuse. Timers, per-cast preparations, potency/source capture, life/floor validation and HP remain with existing owners. |
| Native action cooldowns | Each host actor holds an immutable `Timers` value from `player_cooldowns.gd`. ClassCaster, Game's dodge path and native class effects replace it when actions start, refund or refresh deadlines. | Action admission, class effects, aim, HUD, player snapshots and diagnostics | Preserve host timing and use existing guest projections. Carry the immutable value through actor rollback. |
| Character and class progression | `CharacterProfile` serializes the profile. Host gameplay edits `player.character` through class/loadout commands and publishes guest progress through the existing projection and acknowledgement path. | `class_catalog.gd`, the five connected trees, `affinity_build.gd`, roster and profile serializers | Separate permanent progression commands and build inputs from in-run actor state. Preserve profile IDs, selected nodes and XP scale. |
| Expedition participation and travel | `ExpeditionParticipation` owns run, route, depth, floor, cycle, seed, epoch, participant bindings, return holds and consent. `ExpeditionTransitions` coordinates admitted entry, advance, return, wipe and reset; `PartyTravel` validates gate consent through the same owner. | Gate, combat identities, rewards, ledgers, HUD, network and transactions | Game retains admission RPCs, encounters, terrain reset, settlement and publication. Deepest cleared floor and world drops remain separate reward/game state. Preserve exactly-once travel and guarded rollback across owners. |
| Expedition wallet | `Wallet` in `expedition_wallet.gd` owns each actor’s earned sparks and spent Essence. Game and monster reward settlement install accepted successors. Game supplies the shared floor milestone. | Card rerolls, player HUD and network snapshots | Keep balances derived from one player wallet and the existing floor progression. The monster reward owner retains the separate XP and loot awards. |
| Monster reward receipts | `MonsterRewards` owns frozen awards, delivery and settlement state by encounter epoch and spawn token. Game supplies eligible recipients, deterministic roll callbacks and verified transaction outcomes. | Enemy death handling, wallet credit, profile settlement, guarded departure and encounter reset | Preserve immediate run payouts and checkpoint profile credit as separate operations on the same receipt. Preserve deduplication through rollback and cleanup. |
| Guarded departure settlement | `DepartureSettlement` in `departure_settlement.gd` owns detached settlement records and reconnect holds. Game registers, stages and finalizes them through the existing guarded save route. | Reconnect admission, kill rewards, safe-pouch recovery, Garden return and host exit | Preserve peer and character identity together. Keep frozen kill receipts and the durable save verdict separate from the detached payload. |
| Cumulative graft acquisitions | `ExpeditionGrafts.Ledger` owns the ordered receipts of one actor's run. Game installs the next ledger and debits the selected trophy. | Altar, affinity build resolution, native graft attack capture, local Tab/HUD and owner-only guest details | Keep the ledger as the sole receipt source. Separate it from temporary proc counters in `native_graft_state`, whose lifetimes include fall and floor changes. |
| Upgrade cards | `Ledger` in `affinity_card_ledger.gd` owns each actor’s current affinity offer, completed checkpoint and acquired copies. Game installs a successor and applies its approved cost. | Checkpoint UI, weapon/ability resolution, graft previews and owner-only snapshots | Keep receipt storage in the ledger and active effects derived from the current build. Game still coordinates wallet debits and owns offer revision allocation and checkpoint readiness. |
| Enemies and encounter state | `EncounterRoster` owns host membership, spawn tokens, HP, death confirmation and clearance. EnemyTactics owns the leader position, engagement, deadlines and active boss phase. The roster keeps scout and elite role records. ExpeditionParticipation supplies the floor epoch; Game retains hazard records. | AI, hit testing, rewards, Stage and replicated state | Use scoped target queries and detached packet projections. Keep the encounter epoch, TC01 movement and ranged clearance, and MonsterRewards settlement boundaries explicit. |
| Combat resolution | WeaponAttackQueue owns ordinary delivery; NativeClassActions owns shared Q/R execution; OutgoingHits resolves outgoing contacts. Game coordinates ordered stages, specialist effect queues and guarded transactions. `incoming_player_hit.gd` resolves incoming defense and health outcomes. `combat_action_events.gd` gives each action source and contact identity. `weapon_contact.gd`, class, graft and affinity handlers resolve effects and still access shared actors or enemies. | Simulation, reward settlement, presentation and snapshots | Provide source/target inputs and return combat outcomes. Keep direct hits separate from secondary effects and keep bounded queue, targeting and contact behavior. |
| Garden progression and experimental scene | `Progress` owns durable Garden data, with Heart, crops and reserves made available to Game commands. `experiment_garden.gd` owns the session layout and its projections. | Garden commands, inventory, Stage, guest details and the Garden serializer | Narrow Garden commands and publish scoped views without copying the full world. Preserve stable plot/item IDs, transaction semantics and the distinction between layout and serialized state. |
| Canonical persistence | `GuardedTitleSession` owns session leases, recovery holds and shutdown. `Progress` and `CharacterProfile` serialize; `game_transaction.gd`, `save_batch.gd` and `persistence_operations.gd` coordinate staging, rollback and publication. | Title, Garden commands, expedition settlement, recovery and guest projection | Prefer explicit participants and outcomes over full mutable-object snapshots. Preserve first-error handling, verified abort, uncertainty and cleanup pending as distinct outcomes. |
| Host world publication | `world_replication.gd` owns private fast/details clocks, outgoing sequence and current recovery frame/key. It also owns health revisions/change detection; reentry/session guards stop stale fast, details and health batches. | Physics stage, committed-cleanup publisher and detached sequence diagnostics | `world_replication_source.gd` reads current owners and supplies detached projections/RPC delivery. It owns no actor, wallet or profile state. Incoming ownership is GuestReplication. Source health facts/transport and world adapters remain integration boundaries; no NetworkPresentation runtime remains. |
| Accepted guest state | `guest_replication.gd` owns received buffers, private details, health revisions/flash, sequence/freshness, names and bounded event lifetimes. | Detached UI/aim/diagnostic queries and `GuestReplicationWorld` projection operations | This is received presentation, not host gameplay authority. No second actor roster; generated terrain is delivered to its existing owner. Host health revisions/publication now belong to WorldReplication, as detailed below. |
| Networking and session lifecycle | `NetworkSession` owns transport/role, pending joins, peer admission, character bindings, party slots, registration deadlines and request budgets. `SessionWorld` coordinates authorized profile/scene operations. Game keeps the 25 RPC entry points and builds actor records. | Host command routes, reliable owner recovery, UI and guest projections | Keep participant, reward and profile owners separate. Revoke connection capabilities before closing transport; compare captured bindings on delayed work. `network_contract.gd` owns the explicit wire/capability contract; `world_snapshot.gd` retains protocol 41. |

## AR01 graft ledger

[The ledger](../expedition_grafts.gd) has a run identity and typed receipt array. Each accepted purchase creates a successor, and the prior ledger can still serve as a rollback snapshot. The player dictionary holds one current ledger reference. Its earlier mutable dictionary and the whole-player copy used for previews are gone.

| Interface | Inputs and outputs | Consumers |
|---|---|---|
| `Ledger.new()`, `identity()` | Game assigns the host run serial at departure. Return, extraction and wipe remove the player attachment. Floor travel and revival preserve acquisitions. | Game lifecycle and native graft contact validation |
| `details()`, `resolution()` | Detached receipt details and derived bonuses/adaptations. Changing either result leaves the receipts intact. | `affinity_build.gd`, owner snapshot, local HUD and `run_summary_panel.gd` |
| `altar_view()`, `plan_purchase()` | Current floor, carried trophy counts, parsed request and an evaluator bound to a build and health inputs. A plan contains a successor, receipt and exact cost, or a refusal. The evaluator uses the live class cooldown, weapon and health formulas. | Game builds the altar projection and applies an admitted plan synchronously before sending feedback. |
| `request()`, `decode_request()` | Existing altar request fields and validation. | Altar UI, Game RPC route and co-op fixture |

The graft module went from 19 `game.` accesses and six methods accepting Game to zero of each. One raw dictionary accessor and one speculative whole-player copy path were retired. Previews now derive a bonus projection from the receipts. No second authoritative graft state was added. Guest data remains a projection and the guarded rollback reference is the prior ledger. Native ability and graft callers use the same run identity without accessing receipt storage. Game still owns admission, carried inventory and the synchronous publish boundary. Temporary adaptation counters and other actor fields remain shared.

## AR02 affinity card ledger

[The ledger](../affinity_card_ledger.gd) owns the host’s current offer, rerolls, completed checkpoint and acquired copies for one actor’s expedition. Game holds one current ledger reference. Successful transitions return a successor, so guarded rollback can retain the prior ledger. Floor travel preserves acquired copies. Expedition cleanup removes the attachment.

| Operation | Contract |
|---|---|
| `open_offer()`, `offer()` | Open a checkpoint with host-supplied floor, upgrade seed, revision and resolved stats. Reopening the same floor preserves the offer. Inspect a detached offer with its derived closed status. |
| `plan_choice()` | Validate the floor, revision, choice, eligibility, useful limit and available Essence. Return a successor for an accepted reroll, claim or skip, with an Essence cost for rerolls. Acquired copies preserve their rarity, contribution and acquisition identity. |
| `copies()`, `amount()`, `totals()`, `project_owned()`, `completed_floor()` | Query detached receipts, derived contributions or the last completed floor. Owned contributions come from receipts. The current equipment and effect caps determine active contributions for the Tab and guest views. |

Game keeps network and player admission, gate proximity, the revision sequence and party readiness. It installs the accepted card and wallet successors in the same synchronous command before sending feedback. Rejected choices and rerolls debit no Essence. The ledger’s checkpoint completion replaces the affinity writes to `player.last_offer` and stored offer closure flag. The guest still receives the existing completion projection. Game keeps `checkpoint_ready` for gate and party readiness.

The extracted state and projection code went from 37 `game.` accesses and seven Game parameters to zero. The mutable totals dictionary and writable offer and receipt accessors were removed. [The card adapter](../affinity_cards.gd) formats the picker with `checkpoint_view()` and connects the ledger to combat consumers. The existing Echoing Growth combat adapter still needs enemy, field and world data through three Game parameters and 12 `game.` accesses.

## AR03 expedition wallet

[The wallet](../expedition_wallet.gd) is the sole source for an actor’s earned sparks and spent Essence during an expedition. Each credit or accepted debit returns a successor. Guarded rollback can restore the prior reference with the actor snapshot. Game installs a fresh zero wallet when a run starts or the actor returns. Floor travel and revival preserve the wallet. The public balance is zero outside the Wilds.

| Operation | Contract |
|---|---|
| `credit_sparks()` | Add the sparks from an eligible kill reward. Existing monster reward receipts decide which awards can be applied and when. |
| `plan_debit()` | Check a requested Essence cost against the balance. Game installs the accepted debit with the card reroll before publishing the result. |
| `balance()`, `projection()` | Resolve the balance from the shared cleared-floor milestone, sparks converted at the authored rate and spent Essence. Return detached values for readers. |

Game passes the deepest cleared floor and the current sparks-per-Essence rate explicitly. The wallet stores no second milestone or balance. Host snapshots keep sending the same derived wallet value to guests. Graft purchases still debit trophies. Monster reward receipts freeze Class XP, kill rolls and run drops. Game applies the detached awards. Guarded departure settlement has its own owner.

The old writable `essence_sparks` and `spent` fields are gone. `ExpeditionWallet.ESSENCE_PER_CLEARED_FLOOR` owns the room award and [the combat balance](../game_balance.gd) resolves the configured kill awards and conversion rate. Card and graft owners do not maintain competing currency balances.

## AR04 guarded departure settlement

[Departure settlement](../departure_settlement.gd) holds the detached payload for a guarded host’s disconnected participant. Game passes the peer and character identity separately from a snapshot of the character, projection base, Wilds state, safe-pouch and seed data, and Garden return identifiers. The owner's private copy has a different lifetime from the active actor. A disconnected participant loses the run wallet while their eligible profile progress and safe-pouch cargo await settlement.

| Operation | Contract |
|---|---|
| `register(peer_id, identity, snapshot)` | Register the peer and character with a private copy of the payload. The owner holds the character's reconnect admission while that departure is unresolved. |
| `stage(identities_waiting_on_rewards)` | Give the guarded save caller a detached batch. A departure waits while its matching profile rewards are deferred. |
| `finish(result)` | `COMMITTED` retires staged records. `CLEANUP_PENDING` retains an identity hold after commitment. `RESTORED` keeps the original record for retry after verified rollback. `UNCERTAIN` holds admission without replay. |
| `blocks_admission()`, `status()`, `count()`, `is_empty()`, `contains_peer()`, `has_wild_departure()`, `is_settling()` | Give admission and settlement callers narrow queries for identity and pending work. |

Game keeps session authorization, active actor removal, the final save verdict and effect publication. Game matches a frozen reward against the same peer and character identity for detached settlement, then records the updated profile, recovers the safe pouch and applies the disconnected Garden outcome in the same transaction. Normal extraction and a flush outside an active expedition settle remaining frozen kills before completing departures. A departure can be registered while saving is blocked. Stage and retry still require the existing safe guarded route. The owner operates in host memory with no direct access to Game, save files or networking. A restart uses the existing durable recovery path and cannot replay volatile departure records or kill receipts. The ordinary legacy disconnect route still uses its existing settlement.

AR04 replaced nine raw Game and reward-service accesses to the pending departure dictionary, excluding its declaration. It removed the dictionary and the full-actor alias that it kept. The owner holds authoritative records, including retry and reconnect state. Staged batches belong to the guarded save caller until the verdict. A verified rollback leaves the captured payload available for the next safe attempt.

Use `guarded_expedition` for detached ownership, deferred profile rewards, rollback and retry. `candidate_release_party` covers the disconnect publication boundary over real loopback, including recovery holds. `experimental_terrain_coop` exercises successful disconnect settlement and rejoin.

## AR05 monster reward receipts

[MonsterRewards](../monster_rewards.gd) owns one host-session record per encounter epoch and enemy spawn token. A record freezes each eligible player's class, peer ID and character identity with the rolled part, boss seed, Essence and Class XP. Summon exclusion uses the same receipt identity so it cannot generate rewards on a later death callback. Game supplies candidate eligibility facts and the existing roll callbacks. The owner has no access to actors, Game, save files or networking.

| Operation | Contract |
|---|---|
| `admit_kill()`, `take_run_awards()` | Admit a death once before rolling. Return detached run awards once for immediate experimental payout. |
| `begin_expedition()`, `forfeit_expedition()`, `finish_expedition()` | Freeze pre-entry Class XP for all five classes and monster familiarity by character/run. Claim a voluntary forfeiture once, dropping only that character's pending permanent awards. Close the boundary on extraction, wipe or settled disconnect. These historical counters are not a second current-progress balance; the existing transaction savepoint restores them on confirmed abort. |
| `stage_pending()` | Mark pending receipts as staged and return detached awards for the current settlement. |
| `pending_receipts()`, `pending_count()`, `pending_profile_ids()`, `status()` | Inspect frozen work, track blocked departure rewards and expose receipt phases without sharing mutable records. |
| `savepoint()`, `restore_savepoint()`, `finish_settlement()` | Register changes with the actual guarded and legacy transaction roots. Verified rollback restores work without rerolling. Committed and cleanup-pending results retain deduplication evidence. Uncertain work stays held and is not replayed. |
| `retire_completed()` | Release completed and excluded receipt IDs at the existing encounter boundary while preserving pending work, cleanup holds and uncertainty. |
| `defer_legacy_notices()`, `take_legacy_notices()` | Retain reward notices until the outer legacy transaction can publish them. |

[Game](../game.gd) applies run currency and physical drops, updates eligible live or detached profiles, and publishes the staged notices at the transaction boundary. Experimental play retains immediate run payouts and banks Class XP and familiarity at the existing checkpoints. The guarded and contained persistence routes use one frozen receipt. A peer ID without the matching character identity cannot inherit another player's credit.

AR05 retires three Game collections and sixteen raw accesses across Game and the previous reward utility. The reward owner has zero direct Game reads/writes. Expedition context and travel now belong to ExpeditionParticipation and ExpeditionTransitions. Player admission, XP limits and save publication remain with Game and the established transaction services. No save-schema, protocol or packet-format changes are required. Multiplayer still checks matching build compatibility signatures. The receipt store lives in host memory. A host crash before a verified checkpoint can lose uncommitted Class XP and monster familiarity. A voluntary host exit instead forfeits the host character's run; involuntarily disconnected teammates retain the existing failed-run settlement. Expedition-only drops and Essence are lost with the run. The existing durable journal still reconciles any commit that was in flight.

Both transaction routes return the durability outcome separately from the gameplay return value. On the ordinary route, `save_batch.gd` reads the journal commit marker before cleanup. A confirmed commit can publish rewards and the expedition result even if cleanup fails. A failed write restores gameplay only when the originals are intact or restored and verified. An uncertain write retains its evidence, blocks the session and withholds tentative results.

Use `monster_rewards` for receipt identity, frozen awards and nested legacy rollback, `experimental_terrain` for immediate run payout and deferred permanent progress, `guarded_expedition` for checkpoint rollback and retry, and `candidate_release_party` for disconnect and recovery outcomes over loopback. `guarded_expedition_coop` and `four_players_coop` cover host-owned profile settlement and party payout.

## AR06 native action cooldowns

[Player cooldowns](../player_cooldowns.gd) owns a single immutable timer value on each host actor. It keeps three deadlines in milliseconds for native Q, R and dodge. Game initializes it on admission and replaces it with a ready value when the actor enters an expedition. Floor advance, falls, revival and Garden return preserve the deadlines. Leaving the session removes the actor and its timer. A new expedition resets all three deadlines. The existing guarded and legacy actor savepoints can restore the previous timer value because updates replace the value.

| Operation | Contract |
|---|---|
| `deadline()`, `ready()` | Read a native action's deadline or compare it with the caller's time. |
| `start()`, `refund()`, `ready_by()`, `with_deadline()` | Return a replacement value for a successful cast or dodge, a class refund or an explicit refresh. The caller supplies the resolved duration, reduction or deadline. |
| `skill_seconds()`, `skill_projection()` | Project native Q/R cooldowns in seconds with the existing readiness flags. The host uses these values for its HUD and snapshots. |

Game and the class effect modules replace the same actor value. Class definitions, connected nodes, cards and grafts continue to supply their existing resolved durations, reductions and floors. Host aim checks the deadline owner. Guest aim and HUD use host-projected readiness and remaining seconds, with dodge readiness in the existing owner details. The absolute deadlines stay on the host. Diagnostic deadline values are projections for observation. The save schema and packet formats stay the same. AR06 removed 53 old deadline-key references, including 26 in Game. The former keys have no runtime storage or mutation callers. The original Q and R names remain only in diagnostic projections and their sampling filter.

Core/B recovery, ordinary weapon attack cadence, animation and casting windows, status deadlines and enemy action timers keep their current ownership. Use the five connected-class fixtures for native casts, refunds and dodge refreshes. Use `ability_aim`, `player_hud` and the existing snapshot and loopback checks for readiness presentation.

## AR07 player health

[Player vitals](../player_vitals.gd) owns current HP, resolved native maximum HP and temporary Vital capacity for each host actor. The effective maximum combines the two capacity values. Host actor admission installs one immutable health value. Damage, healing and changes in capacity replace it. The save transaction can restore an older actor dictionary and its prior health value without sharing mutable HP state.

| Operation | Contract |
|---|---|
| `hp()`, `native_max_hp()`, `temporary_hp()`, `max_hp()` | Read current HP or one of the three capacity values. |
| `with_capacity()`, `with_native_max_hp()`, `with_hp()` | Replace the chosen values and clamp current HP to the effective capacity. Capacity increases alone grant no healing. |
| `healed()`, `damaged()`, `refilled()` | Apply HP changes through replacement values. Damage keeps its existing whole-HP accounting. |
| `current_hp()`, `maximum_hp()` | Read the live host value or the guest's projected scalar fields for presentation. |

Game and the existing balance helpers resolve weapon, class, card, graft and expedition capacity inputs. The existing life-loss amount remains a separate actor field for revival eligibility and maximum HP. Damage receipts and network revisions keep their existing owners.

Host snapshots project HP and effective maximum HP into the existing reliable and fast fields. The guest keeps that projected data and reconciles the two channels by the existing revision. HUD, party, spectator, stats and diagnostic readers use the same host value or guest projection. Damage flash still follows the existing damage sequence. The component stays out of packets and character saves.

This removes 37 raw player HP or capacity member accesses in Game, along with its two raw health fields and the separate Vitality bonus cache. Lifecycles still choose explicit refills where gameplay calls for them, including revival and Garden return.

Use `combat_vitals_baseline` and the actual combat and support fixtures for capacity, damage and healing. Use `guarded_expedition` for rollback, `party_hud_gates_coop` and `diagnostics_coop` for host/guest presentation, and `network_presentation` and `world_snapshot_codec` for stale-frame precedence.

## AR08 player lifecycle

[Player lifecycle](../player_lifecycle.gd) owns each host actor's downed state and life generation. Game admits actors; [expedition transitions](../expedition_transitions.gd) coordinates entry and return, and [expedition life](../expedition_life.gd) handles fall and revival through replacement values. Admission, revival, expedition entry and Garden return allocate a new generation. Falling and waiting for the party preserve the existing generation. The savepoint restores the previous actor value on rollback. The allocator never reuses a generation reserved by an aborted transition.

| Operation | Contract |
|---|---|
| `start_life()` | Create an active life with a new host generation. |
| `fall()` | Preserve the generation and mark the life downed. ExpeditionParticipation now owns the separate post-return party hold; `PlayerLifecycle.fallen()` projects that hold for existing gameplay consumers. |
| `clear_inactive()` | Give an inactive actor a fresh life when the party releases the wait. |
| `generation()`, `matches()` | Capture and validate the source or recipient life for delayed host combat work. |
| `fallen()` | Read the host lifecycle value or the existing fallen field on a guest projection. |

Delayed class, Core, affinity, and graft work compare against the captured host generation where their existing source policies require ownership. Falling alone does not invalidate an accepted Ward or Omen whose source policy allows it to finish. A new life or departure invalidates that source. Floor and run ownership still use their existing identities. This replaces 17 direct reads or writes of the fallen field in Game. Game still coordinates expedition participation through `wild`. The `lost_health` field drives the revival HP penalty, and the existing Core and aimed class requests retain it in their wire compatibility fingerprint.

Fast and reliable snapshots retain their public fallen and HP-penalty fields. Movement, HUD, spectator and diagnostics read the host state or the guest projection as appropriate. Movement retains its existing floor/seed/Wilds/fallen epoch. The private generation stays with the host actor.

Use `loot_revival` and its co-op fixture for lifecycle, `guarded_expedition` for rollback, `cr02_combat_contracts` and the connected-class and affinity fixtures for delayed work. Use `world_snapshot_codec`, `network_movement_coop` and the party HUD and diagnostics fixtures to verify the projections.

## AR09 encounter roster

[EncounterRoster](../encounter_roster.gd) owns the active leader and scout membership, current HP, spawn tokens, confirmed defeat, and the floor-clear predicate. Game creates the roster and begins each floor. A scout spawn plan specifies initial HP and consumes its reserved token at registration. Its tactics dictionary supports movement, attack preparation, and status timing. Slot numbers retain the existing packet order. The floor's leader occupies target `-1`. Delayed work also carries the existing encounter epoch.

| Operation | Contract |
|---|---|
| `begin_floor()`, `register_scout()`, `clear_scouts()` | Start the next active roster, fill or replace a seat, or clear scout membership. Fresh occupants use fresh identities. |
| `hp()`, `alive()`, `token()`, `position()`, `cleared()` | Resolve narrow structural queries. Use a captured token for delayed scout work. Since AR10, Game resolves the leader position through EnemyTactics. |
| `apply_damage()`, `set_hp()` | Apply resolved damage against the expected occupant. Set HP explicitly for setup, recovery and other authored overrides. |
| `death_pending()`, `confirm_death()`, `despawn()` | Confirm an eligible death once. Escape retires an occupant without awarding a kill. Game coordinates effects and passes frozen death inputs to MonsterRewards. |
| `scout_view()`, `scout_pack()`, `scout_codes()` | Send detached data to outside callers. Fast host frames retain protocol 41's scalar order and size. Guest presentation uses received projections. |
| `savepoint()`, `restore_savepoint()` | Capture or restore roster health, membership and defeat during guarded rollback. The token allocator retains consumed identities across rollback. |

Specialist movement, telegraphs, roles, boss phases, and status effects use mutable tactical records. Those records carry no authoritative scout HP or spawn token after registration. AR10 moves the leader fields and behavior out of Game. Game retains hit math, effect queues, floor routing, and settlement orchestration.

Use `encounter_roster` for owner invariants, `combat_animation` for slot reuse during a contact batch, `boss_phase_fights` for bounded summons, `monster_rewards` for receipts, `guarded_expedition` for rollback, and `world_snapshot_codec` for protocol shape and stale updates. `boss_coop`, `guarded_expedition_coop`, `experimental_terrain_coop`, and `four_players_coop` exercise the real integration.

## AR10 enemy tactical state and behavior

[EnemyTactics](../enemy_tactics.gd) is the host owner for the leader's position, engagement, attack deadlines, adaptation and plan memory, and active boss kind and phase. The gate-elite role record lives on the leader member in [EncounterRoster](../encounter_roster.gd). Game coordinates encounter reset, damage, rewards, persistence and publication. ExpeditionParticipation owns the encounter epoch. Both transaction paths restore the tactical owner alongside the roster. Guests project accepted protocol-41 leader fields through `EnemyTactics.project()`.

| Path | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| [leader_behavior.gd](../leader_behavior.gd) | Routes a floor leader to the Heart's Briar, a gate elite, or the boss dispatcher. Owns Briar decisions and release. |
| [scout_behavior.gd](../scout_behavior.gd) | Advances regular and summoned scouts and releases prepared scout and gate-elite attacks. |
| [enemy_formation.gd](../enemy_formation.gd) | Finds escorts, arranges formations, and advances support follow behavior. |
| [combat_director.gd](../combat_director.gd) and [enemy_attack.gd](../enemy_attack.gd) | Select targets and navigate. Author aim, collision, telegraphs, and attack geometry. |

The ordinary scout attack path passes a read-only value observation to `choose_attack()`. Game and live actor dictionaries stay on the host side. `apply_attack_intent()` validates the encounter epoch, roster token, target life, current readiness, role, reach and sight before locking the existing telegraph. Specialized movements and boss plans keep their local decision paths. Shared combat and specialist code still access EnemyTactics fields and roster role dictionaries through Game.

Use `regular_enemy_responses` for valid and stale attack intents, `enemy_roster` for role telegraphs, `boss_phase_fights` and `boss_lairs` for phase changes, `guarded_expedition` for tactical rollback, and `boss_coop` with `four_players_coop` for replicated outcomes.


## AR11 enemy attack execution

[ScoutBehavior](../scout_behavior.gd) selects an ordinary attack and submits the read-only observation for host validation. [EnemyAttack](../enemy_attack.gd) locks the existing plan, carries the floor epoch, scout slot and spawn token, and records the intended peer and life generation. It claims a due plan before effect callbacks run. Cancellation and stale floor or source identity invalidate the plan. Volley repeats schedule another beat of the same locked attack. Scout warnings use the same committed geometry, and the roster still provides the gate elite's role record to its warning and release paths.

[EnemyAttackHits](../enemy_attack_hits.gd) resolves area, swept-charge, and sector contacts using the bound field and detached target positions. Its world adapter rechecks source identity and player life before submitting a validated contact to [IncomingPlayerHit](../incoming_player_hit.gd). A replacement life for the intended peer is excluded. Other live players can still enter the locked footprint. Delayed scout hazards retain the commitment in [boss_encounters.gd](../boss_encounters.gd) while using the existing hazard schedule.

Elite movement, boss phase scripts, hazards, class effects, and the lure pull retain their specialized dispatch. The protocol-41 projection keeps the existing wire layout.

Use `regular_enemy_responses` for locked-plan deadline, replay, target changes, stale floor and actor identity, and delayed hazards. Use `combat_vitals_baseline` for defensive ordering, `projectile_terrain` for TC01, `gate_elites_coop` and `boss_coop` for replicated warnings, and `guarded_expedition` with `four_players_coop` for rollback and multiplayer.

## AR12 incoming player hits

[IncomingPlayerHit.resolve_world()](../incoming_player_hit.gd) accepts the peer, captured life generation, hit time, authored damage, Slow duration, and attack origin and classification. It returns an outcome, actual HP loss, and whether a damage record was written. Its recipient adapter revalidates the host and current life and gives the resolver detached combat facts. The recipient adapter still binds one actor record from Game for the existing status, class and affinity hooks. Game supplies the guarded transaction and combat feedback. The resolver owns the order of those steps.

The resolver checks hit grace and consumes eligible Guard or Ward. Unblocked damage uses the existing HP conversion, Water and Warden mitigation, and Bark absorption. [`PlayerHitConditions.State.after_hit()`](../player_hit_conditions.gd) applies ordinary Slow and grace. IncomingPlayerHit asks the Omen owner to consume Quiet Ground only when its prevention is eligible; CombatStatus only projects the resulting status. Surviving hits can trigger the existing Oracle, Frost, Fire, and Bark responses. [PlayerVitals.apply_damage()](../player_vitals.gd) stores HP while [ExpeditionLife](../expedition_life.gd) handles falling with [PlayerLifecycle](../player_lifecycle.gd). A guarded lethal outcome commits before deferred Bark work and returns an uncommitted result on refusal or rollback. The raw `apply_health_loss()` operation shares HP application and settlement. Health and rollback fixtures use it to exercise HP changes independently of defenses.

Use `combat_vitals_baseline` for defenses, simultaneous hit grace and stale lives, `guarded_expedition` for lethal rollback, and `regular_enemy_responses` for scheduled contacts and replay. `ct01_warden`, `ct04_reaper`, `ct05_oracle`, the focused affinity fixtures, and card and graft fixtures cover the existing reactions. `gate_elites_coop`, `boss_coop`, `combat_readability_coop` and `four_players_coop` cover visible outcomes and multiplayer.

## AR13 expedition participation and travel
