## Natural Wilds flora (WF01)

A living expedition participant can harvest one named seed of the plant’s own species from a nearby specimen, even during combat. The host fixes the species and position when the floor opens. Every participant can harvest that same species once. A successful harvest changes only that character’s carried inventory and claim. A successful extraction, Returning Root delivery or protected pouch recovery puts that seed directly in the character’s planting stock. The Garden earns its usual 4 Knowledge once for the first secured seed of that species. Abandoned-cache seeds remain mysterious until identified at the altar.

| Editing task | Source and rule |
|---|---|
| Botanical identity | `seed_catalog.gd` defines the 24 stable species, rarities, preferred biomes, minimum ordinary encounters and mature forms. Garden crops and Wilds specimens share these definitions. |
| Spawn and selection | `wild_flora_generation.gd` owns the provisional 40% floor roll and six global-encounter rarity bands. Eligible rarities receive their relative weights. Species below their own minimum encounter are excluded. Within a rarity, the preferred biome gives a species 1.5× weight. The floor seed, route, local depth and global encounter seed an independent random stream. |
| First two encounters | `WildFlora.starter` asks `InventoryModel.garden_or_party_has_seed` about Garden discoveries, available stock, unidentified reserves and present characters’ carried/pouched seeds. Floor 1 prioritizes missing Thorn Bloom, floor 2 missing Lantern Moss. Either can supply the other missing starter. In any starting biome a missing starter bypasses the chance and biome rolls. A Garden with both covered uses ordinary odds. Co-op gets one shared specimen. Individual pickup history still belongs to the separate cache. |
| Floor placement | `Game.start_encounter` supplies actual enemy formation positions to `Battlefield.generate_flora`. It searches room margins and trail shoulders connected to the entrance through existing certified navigation, including side branches. A missing starter can also use dry ground within reach of a certified trail when the ordinary candidate positions are all occupied. It excludes water, cover, obstructing foliage, entrances, gates, stations, cache and spawn positions. Ordinary, elite and guardian procedural floors are eligible. Authored boss arenas and practice maps supply no specimens. |
| Collection and recovery | `wild_flora.gd` holds one floor record and its successful collector identity. A physical specimen accepts one party-wide claim. `Game.harvest_wild_flora` checks host authority, session binding, live participation and write readiness. The owner checks specimen identity, 1.55 m reach and a clear segment before calling `InventoryModel.admit_wild_harvest` for the successful collector. A failed admission leaves the claim open. The transaction savepoint restores inventory and claims together on verified rollback. |
| Network and display | The targeted harvest RPC uses the existing character/life/world action stamp. The specimen travels in reliable owner details with the same claimed state for every participant. Protocol 41 retains its compact layout and the handshake requires matching harvest and Returning Root capabilities. `Stage.show_wild_flora` uses the Garden’s mature crop geometry, switches to a smaller young model after the accepted harvest for everyone, and respects local sight distance. `ui/gameplay/world_guidance.gd` supplies the nearby interaction prompt. |
| Other seed sources | The ordinary encounter-clear seed bundle has been retired. The separate 20% ordinary cache opportunity keeps its own eligibility, random roll and introductory protections. Boss Ancient Mystery Seeds keep their existing reward and identification rules. |
| Playtest coverage | `tests/wild_flora_generation.gd` covers depth/biome selection, map determinism and reachable placement. `tests/wild_flora_harvest.gd` exercises actual guarded inventory admission, extraction, altar identification, rollback, pouch, wipe, abandonment and Returning Root deposits. `tests/wild_flora_coop.gd` and `tests/wild_flora_party.gd` verify competing requests for a single physical specimen, shared claimed state and independent Returning Root allowances over loopback. |

Extraction banks carried seeds. A wipe recovers the safe pouch. Voluntary abandonment forfeits carried and pouched items. A previously committed root delivery stays in the Garden. Specimens and their claims are floor-local. Disconnect and readmission follow the existing expedition rules.

### WF01 generation sample

The deterministic headless sample used 90 ordinary maps per depth band, evenly divided across Grove, Mire and Hollow. It exercised actual terrain and spawn anchors with the introductory override disabled. Of 540 maps, 228 passed the 40% roll and 227 placed a reachable specimen (42.04% of maps). One successful roll lacked a suitable ordinary site. The independent starter check placed the missing species on 192 of 192 introductory maps. Twenty-one paired game encounters also checked the final enemy positions as exclusions.

| Global encounter | Specimens / 90 | Common | Uncommon | Rare | Epic | Mythic |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1–5 | 38 | 38 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| 6–15 | 37 | 26 | 11 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| 16–30 | 38 | 15 | 18 | 5 | 0 | 0 |
| 31–50 | 38 | 9 | 11 | 13 | 5 | 0 |
| 51–59 | 42 | 5 | 10 | 14 | 11 | 2 |
| 60+ (sampled to 90) | 34 | 5 | 11 | 8 | 6 | 4 |

| Rarity | Specimens by species |
|---|---|
| Common | Thorn Bloom 44, Lantern Moss 35, Ember Fern 19 |
| Uncommon | Veilcap 15, Cinder Vine 15, Dewcap 11, Ironroot 20 |
| Rare | Bloodbriar 5, Ghost Fern 3, Storm Reed 6, Moon Lotus 2, Frostbell 3, Whispervine 7, Mirror Mint 5, Gravemoss 5, Tide Lily 4 |
| Epic | Star Orchid 7, Void Willow 4, Sun Spire 2, Hush Orchid 6, Dream Poppy 3 |
| Mythic | Eclipse Root 3, Worldseed 2, Crownroot 1 |

To repeat the 540-map sample, run the registered `wild_flora_generation` fixture with `MYSTICAL_FLORA_SAMPLE_SIZE=30`. For visual playtesting, launch the branch with `tools/launch_experimental_terrain.ps1 -GodotExe <path-to-Godot>`. This source launcher creates a fresh disposable profile each time.

## Single modern Garden and Wilds terrain

The generated Garden is the default on ordinary title/world entry, alongside the modern Wilds, all four boss lairs and the Heart route. The previous opt-in Garden was already in Git ancestry; its startup flag and the special-floor fallback kept those changes from being the normal experience. The `--experimental-terrain` name is retained only for the verified isolated-save launcher/fixture contract. It no longer selects a generator. `Experiment.network_identity()` reports the same terrain contract for both source launch routes.

| Editing task / owner | Authoritative path and interface |
|---|---|
| Active Wilds floor and combat queries | [`battlefield.gd`](../battlefield.gd): `configure`, `occupied`, `dash`, `slide`, `navigate`, `can_spawn`, `projectile_block_fraction`, `projectile_endpoint`, `combat_sight`. Every floor has the modern `ground` surface and `navigation_graph`; there is no `procedural` selector or old raster grid. A clear ranged segment uses the established `INF` sentinel and returns its requested finite endpoint. |
| Seeded ordinary terrain | [`assets/terrain_m02/wilds_preview_generation.gd`](../assets/terrain_m02/wilds_preview_generation.gd), the existing biome/geography/route definitions, and [`wilds_play_surface.gd`](../assets/terrain_m02/wilds_play_surface.gd). A bounded generation failure uses an explicitly authored safe clearing through this same surface/navigation pipeline. |
| Boss and Heart ground | [`assets/terrain_m02/wilds_boss_layout.gd`](../assets/terrain_m02/wilds_boss_layout.gd) reserves the existing lair sizes, approach, exit, leader and utility landmarks. Nest rib geometry still comes from [`boss_nest_layout.gd`](../boss_nest_layout.gd). Specialized boss AI, damage, hazards and phase timing remain in their existing owners. |
| Garden terrain, construction and offerings | [`assets/terrain_m02/experimental/experiment_garden.gd`](../assets/terrain_m02/experimental/experiment_garden.gd) integrates the existing generated Garden. [`garden_expansion.gd`](../assets/terrain_m02/garden_expansion.gd) owns reclaimed land; [`garden_construction.gd`](../assets/terrain_m02/garden_construction.gd) owns physical placements; `garden_offerings.gd` quotes both immediate rewards and validates free stock. Research grants permission; it does not silently replace player-chosen reclamation with automatic land grants. |
| Garden presentation and native services | [`stage.gd`](../stage.gd) presents generated terrain and the native landmark layer. Bed, gate, altar and station consumers query their actual positions. Research invalidates landmark presentation. The native Living Archive retains its on-site interaction because it has no construction replacement. |
| Garden canopy palette and density | [`garden_canopy_catalog.gd`](../garden_canopy_catalog.gd) owns colours, two staggered crown layers, edge-tree dimensions and ten-metre chunk size. [`garden_canopy.gd`](../garden_canopy.gd) owns render resources and receives the detached sampled boundary/reveal. Whole crowns shrink out of reclaimed ground and complete edge trees move outward. There is no opaque backing mesh, vertical skirt or fragment clipping against the clearing. |
| Garden foliage models | [`assets/garden/canopy/`](../assets/garden/canopy/) contains CC0 Quaternius leaf crowns plus complete Maple/Birch edge trees. Crowns retain a centred one-metre horizontal span; complete trees retain a grounded origin, one-metre height and separate `Trunk`/`Crown` nodes. Preserve UVs; no collision or rig is required. [`sources.json`](../assets/garden/canopy/sources.json) records pinned donors/hashes; rebuild with Blender and [`tools/build_garden_canopy_assets.py`](../tools/build_garden_canopy_assets.py). |
| Single Garden Heart | Edit `assets/source/garden_heart.blend` and export `assets/terrain_m02/garden_heart.glb`. Preserve `HeartStage1` through `HeartStage5`, bark material names and flow UVs. The live Garden runs `terrain_heart_preview.gd` with `sculpt_only = true`. Each completed technology adds 1.8% of the authored height and 0.8% of the authored width. `heart_growth_visuals.gd::sync` updates the sculpt. The old procedural Heart builder, ending scenery and final-stage UI icon were removed. `heart_visuals.gd::station` supplies Garden stations. The standalone terrain preview can still display its comparison meshes. |
| Rounded reclamation joins | [`garden_boundary.gd`](../garden_boundary.gd) rounds inward seams using a 0.75 m round closing and unions the result with the original polygon. Existing land is never cut away. `garden_expansion.gd::proposal` validates the rounded result against the existing terrain, construction and route rules. This rounds concave joins, rather than shaving every convex tip or rewriting saved geometry. |
| Shared geometry and cover | [`wilds_play_surface.gd`](../assets/terrain_m02/wilds_play_surface.gd) and [`terrain_feature_geometry.gd`](../assets/terrain_m02/terrain_feature_geometry.gd) retain separate actor movement, projectile, combat sight and ground-support semantics. Water and authored low rubble remain movement-only blockers; solid cover and arena exits stop shots. |
| Neural navigation | [`mob_sim/mob_nav_world.gd`](../mob_sim/mob_nav_world.gd) bakes a conservative cell footprint from actual modern occupancy. The trophy thief and Arena practice bot use the existing certified route graph, rather than a removed legacy grid. |

Retired runtime: `wilds_layout.gd`, `walkable_surface.gd`, the `experiment_wilds.gd` subclass/fallback, and Stage's circular Garden/old Wilds ground and generator builders. Reusable native landmark/effect art is retained. No extra player/enemy roster, currency, reward or terrain authority was introduced.

The active Garden forest uses overlapping upper/lower leaf crowns and 72 complete, grounded trees in two irregular rows around the clearing. It replaces the former 768 whole-tree background and the first pass's flat infill/skirt. Background foliage is spatially batched; edge meshes use native bounds. Reclamation retires covered crowns without slicing their geometry. Unrevealed terrain props retain their authoritative footprints while their presentation stays hidden. The older enclosure renderer remains a standalone authoring preview, not a selectable game fallback. Weapon/locomotion animation is unchanged.

`garden_forest_barrier.gd::bind(state, false)` supplies the existing movement boundary without constructing its old tangle meshes. Construction now calls `thicket_centres()` for detached forest-clearance positions instead of reading mesh transforms. The same placement formula serves previews and queries; focused tests compare the returned positions exactly with the former rendered positions. Clearing ownership, movement and construction restrictions retain their existing authorities.

Focused coverage: `garden_canopy` checks complete models, two leaf layers, absent backing/skirt, grounded tree origins, boundary interpolation and culling. `garden_construction` checks equivalent clearances with rendering disabled. `heart_growth_visual`/`heart_progression` check imported-only growth through every enabled research unlock and reload; `experimental_terrain` checks regular updates cannot resurrect the old Heart. Garden growth/return and terrain co-op fixtures protect the existing integration. Visual review uses Godot 4.7.2 close views, five authored Heart stages and expanded layouts with disposable saves. Automated loopback does not establish separate-PC/internet verification or human visual approval.

`adopt_recorded_beds()` gives an already-owned saved crop slot a construction placement at its historical position without purchasing it again. Crop content remains solely in Progress. Its actual construction footprint supplies collision; scenery reservations cannot introduce a second invisible collider. Unresearched observation reservations do not become essential construction-door requirements or claim future land. Missing historical layout data uses this bounded adoption; the current durable layout contract is documented under Saved Garden development below.

Compatibility deliberately advances the terrain signature to `modern-terrain-3`. Protocol 41's compact snapshot layout, RPC signatures and `MysticalGarden` NodePath are preserved. Old terrain implementations are refused before admission. The cutover also corrects a regression inherited from the persistence-session refactor: Void resolves its own pending displacement dictionary, guarded separately by persistence state. Its damage, allowance and arbitration values are unchanged.

Focused ownership checks are `modern_terrain_cutover`, `terrain_generation`, `exploration_maps`, `path_junctions`, `projectile_terrain`, `experimental_terrain`, construction/expansion/offerings, CT01–CT05, EC01, grafts, reward forfeiture and guarded transactions. Real loopback controls include matching/definitions mismatch and RPC contracts, terrain reconstruction, developed-Garden return/rejoin, boss phases, Void and the four-player card/combat/extraction fixture with overflow refusal. Modern obstruction fixtures use `tests/support/terrain_case.gd`; guarded station cases physically place researched structures through `tests/support/garden_case.gd`.

The shared Game/terrain integration affects the planner's full 223-suite dependency set. Only the explicitly selected current interaction checks are refreshed here; this is not fresh all-suite or packaged release evidence. Earlier failed runs remain retained. No production save activation, real-save migration, remote push or release export is part of this integration. The AC-06 deployment gate and host-process-loss limitations remain separate concerns. Local loopback is not separate-PC or internet verification.

Final integration measurements against the starting main (`12e5a153b23bc91f06f41372ad59b75e08c625d6`), using total lines, top-level `func` declarations and plain/`@onready` variables:

| File | Before: lines / functions / variables | Integrated: lines / functions / variables |
|---|---:|---:|
| `game.gd` | 4,487 / 258 / 154 | 4,099 / 251 / 101 |
| `stage.gd` | 1,646 / 58 / 60 | 1,262 / 46 / 53 |
| `battlefield.gd` | 379 / 22 / 19 | 264 / 21 / 20 |

All 45 selected source suites pass at their final input keys (57 unit/peer verdicts); all 106 validation/deployment-tool self-tests pass. Matching-build guests pass authoritative action readiness; the mismatched-definition pair records zero authenticated guests, one host player and zero ready guests. The four-player control passes its fifth-client refusal. Arena verification separately passes 49 checks and both independent local network peers, with the explicitly recorded untrained fixture. One earlier Windows temporary-directory rename failure passed its isolated recheck and the complete tool rerun; the initial failure is retained rather than relabelled.

The merged development branch also retains its already-approved `expedition_abandonment_forfeiture:1` negotiated capability and the newer AC-06 deployment/refusal checks. The activation constant remains false. Those preserved improvements are distinct from changing production activation or the protocol-41 transport/schema bytes.

The local `recovery/modern-owners-before-terrain-cutover-20261118` tag additionally preserves merge `411bccbe58af1c9663b0981371c0aa772e86225d`, including the newly integrated owners/UI with the pre-cutover terrain runtime. The original main recovery tag and all older protected versions remain intact.


## Remaining affinity card families (CE04)

Storm is implemented first: ten additions, **111 total definitions** at its checkpoint. Card values and explicit CE01 requirements remain authoritative in `affinity_card_catalog.gd`; `storm_affinity.gd` consumes them through the existing Core, Form and mapped Catalyst events. `basic_attack_timing.gd` composes each family's temporary attack-speed contribution for both real launches and reliable owner/Stats presentation; it owns no state.

| Storm card | Common / Uncommon / Rare / Epic contribution | Actual scope / consumer |
|---|---|---|
| Extended Circuit | 1 / 2 / 3 / 4 extra targets | Core normal chain |
| Long Arc | 0.25 / 0.40 / 0.55 / 0.70 m | Core per-jump reach |
| Live Wire | 2 / 3 / 4 / 5 damage | Form electrical arc |
| Ionized Rhythm | 4 / 6 / 8 / 10% for 2 s | Form actual damage prepares basic attack speed; existing weapon timing floor |
| Charged Casting | 3 / 5 / 7 / 9 damage | Existing Catalyst discharge, burst and recipient charge |
| Relay Pulse | 1 / 2 / 3 / 4 targets | Existing Catalyst discharge/recipient-charge capabilities only |
| Spellcharge | 3 / 5 / 7 / 9 damage | Actual mapped class response prepares next electrical discharge for 4 s, once |
| Grounded Current | 25 / 40 / 55 / 70% | Unused normal chain opportunities retain native 0.7 falloff and add to primary hit |
| Conductive Reach | 0.20 / 0.30 / 0.40 / 0.50 m | Functioning Storm slot's per-jump reach |
| Conductor | Unique: one 45% fork per normal node, one 35% return | Core only; finite unvisited fork targets and original surviving spawn return within actual jump reach and clear projectile line |

These are provisional tuning values. Return damage uses starting discharge potency before Grounded Current's primary-only addition. Forks cannot fork. Extra jumps, reach and unused-potential conversion make ordinary Storm useful without Conductor. Source life, connection binding, floor epoch and enemy spawn identities remain validated. There is no added Catalyst response type or extra ordinary contact.

Storm verification: `ce04_storm`, `ce04_storm_coop`, `ce04_storm_party`, native Storm, EC01, CE01 eligibility, weapon queue and guarded gameplay transactions passed (8 suites / 13 unit/peer verdicts). The independent local four-player case includes fifth-client refusal, floor continuity, extraction and rejoin. Evidence: `.godot/validation/final-four-ce04-storm-final-20261120-221435/summary.json`. Local loopback is not internet or separate-PC verification. Planner impact is all 264 current suites due to the shared contract/catalog; this focused checkpoint does not refresh broad release evidence. Production persistence activation remains disabled.


### Spirit: stored movement and independent phantoms

Spirit adds fifteen cards, **126 total definitions** at its checkpoint. `player_cooldowns.gd::Timers` owns capacity and a sequential queue of captured recharge durations with one next-recovery deadline. Immutable replacements preserve rollback. `resolved_combat_build.gd` derives capacity and recharge from the locked build; its immutable resolved-potency cache avoids repeatedly resolving the same content for every echo. The provisional ordinary dash recharge is **2,200 ms**, previously 850 ms; its distance, speed and lack of invulnerability are unchanged. Quick Footing remains 45/70/95/120 ms per copy. Flat native/card reductions retain their 350 ms floor **before** Spirit efficiency; divide the resulting duration by `1 + (Fleet + prepared Invocation efficiency)/100`. Four Epic Quick Footing and four Epic Fleet copies produce 344 ms recovery before native class modifiers. A Common of each produces 1,539 ms. Newly acquired charge capacity is ready immediately; spent charges recover one at a time. Native instant-dodge restoration restores one recovering charge.

| Spirit card | Common / Uncommon / Rare / Epic | Actual consumer |
|---|---|---|
| Far Step | 0.25 / 0.40 / 0.55 / 0.70 m | Phantom Step travel and preview |
| Persistent Traces | 150 / 250 / 350 / 450 ms | Core pursuer trace |
| Ghostcut | 2 / 3 / 4 / 5 damage | Native movement-qualified Form follow-up |
| Flowing Edge | 150 / 250 / 350 / 450 ms | Live self Spiritstride extension once per ordinary attack |
| Spectral Tempo | 6 / 9 / 12 / 15% for 2 s | Each accepted voluntary dash or movement skill grants an independent additive attack-speed bonus. Each expires separately; the weapon timing floor still applies. |
| Veiled Casting | 3 / 5 / 7 / 9 percentage points | Catalyst stride movement speed |
| Flowing Invocation | 20 / 30 / 40 / 50% efficiency | Actual mapped class response prepares next spent charge for 4 s |
| Wraith’s Followthrough | 2 / 3 / 4 / 5 damage | Offensive class response prepares first primary basic contact; launch within 4 s |
| Untethered Motion | 250 / 400 / 550 / 700 ms | Own native Spirit stride/trace/follow lifetimes |
| Evasive Spirit | 4 / 6 / 8 / 10% efficiency for 1 s | Direct incoming HP divided by `1 + efficiency/100`, before Bark; no invulnerability |
| Stored Momentum | 1 / 2 / 3 / 4 charges | Existing native cooldown owner |
| Fleet Recovery | 40 / 60 / 80 / 100% efficiency | Sequential dash recovery |
| Lingering Echoes | 500 / 750 / 1,000 / 1,250 ms | Independent prepared lifetimes; requires owned Mirrorstep or Wraithcast |
| Mirrorstep | Unique: 3 s lifetime, 40% weapon/Form potency | All prepared echoes release simultaneously through `weapon_attack_queue.gd` |
| Wraithcast | Unique: 4 s lifetime, 40% effect potency | All eligible departure-position shadows cast once through `spirit_replicas.gd` |

These values are provisional and authored only in `affinity_card_catalog.gd`. `spirit_affinity.gd` owns the lightweight preparations, current source/binding/life/epoch checks and cleanup; the card ledger alone owns acquisition. Ordinary walking and forced motion never create phantoms. Basic attacks consume only echoes; compatible activated Core/class casts consume only shadows. Neither replica repeats native cooldowns, HP spending, character movement, Catalyst or graft procs. There is no fixed echo/shadow count cap. Passive counts and stored charges use existing reliable owner details/HUD; no compact snapshot layout or RPC signature changes.

`spirit_replicas.gd::class_supported/build_supported` is also the authoritative executable eligibility contract. Supported class portions are opening damage/healing and Ward protection. Native learned-node side effects, traps, persistent fields and character movement are not copied. Supported Core portions: Chain Spark contact/discharge, Ironbark protection and Vital Bloom capacity/regeneration. Other Core families remain explicitly unsupported; an unsupported cast retains shadows. A movement-only Core plus trap-only class cannot receive Wraithcast. The card Details and Stats presentation state the compatibility. Mirrorstep uses native scaled leaf Form effects; no extra ordinary class/graft/Harvest ownership is granted.

Spirit verification: 16 focused suites pass, including actual class/Core copying, all nine Form leaf types, native Spirit, CE01, resolved builds, weapon ownership, original class casting, CE03 families, Oracle/Reaper defenses, projectile terrain, hit conditions and guarded gameplay transactions. Independent two-/four-player runs verify actual dash RPCs, both phantoms, reliable charges, duplicate class requests, normal card/graft/floor/extraction/rejoin flow and fifth-player refusal. Evidence: `.godot/validation/final-four-ce04-spirit-ready-20261120-223442/summary.json`. The synthetic four-player stress case retains 128 echoes plus 128 shadows and creates 132 weapon records, no new player actors: approximately 35 ms total preparation, 10 ms queue admission and 7 ms resolution on this machine. These are headless local measurements, not a manual frame-rate guarantee.

A separately attempted `guarded_lifecycle` fixture remains failed at its pre-existing `preferences` binding assertion: it assumes an old B-bound attack can migrate Core to X, already assigned by CE02 card controls. Both that fixture and `app_settings.gd` are unchanged from the CE03 baseline. Its original engine-error log is retained under `final-four-ce04-spirit-final-20261120-223303`. This is not a passing lifecycle/save acceptance claim. Current guarded transaction coverage passes; production AC-06 activation remains false and host-process-loss limitations remain unresolved.


### Void: actual overlapping fields and targeted destruction

Void adds eleven cards, completing CE04's three families at **137 definitions**. `void_affinity.gd` remains the native damage/control consumer. `combat_effect_timeline.gd` owns concurrent lingering Wells and Singularity fields, and `native_effects_world.gd` validates their source life/binding/world and advances them. There is no additional field ledger. Ordinary unmodified Gravity Well keeps its existing Core lifecycle. Acquired Rift Shear reserves an actual 9-second gathering field at Core admission and retains the original one-time 1.08-second collapse; its normal 7.8-second cooldown permits solo overlap. Admission into the existing 24-record technical effect budget is explicit: failure spends neither native nor acquired-action cooldown.

| Void card | Common / Uncommon / Rare / Epic | Actual consumer |
|---|---|---|
| Widening Well | 0.25 / 0.40 / 0.55 / 0.70 m | Gravity Well gathering/collapse radius and preview |
| Deepening Gravity | 20 / 30 / 40 / 50% | Well step and per-window gathering strength; existing resistance/arbitration retained |
| Condensed Edge | 2 / 3 / 4 / 5 damage | First primary basic contact against actual field coverage or recent successful Void displacement |
| Compression Rhythm | 100 / 150 / 200 / 250 ms | Native 1,850 ms Form response interval |
| Grasping Invocation | 0.10 / 0.15 / 0.20 / 0.25 m | Existing offensive Catalyst inward pull; native displacement retains priority |
| Dense Casting | 3 / 5 / 7 / 9 damage | Actual mapped class compression/repulsion |
| Dark Echo | 3 / 5 / 7 / 9 damage | Real class response prepares one next native compression target within 4 s; replicas cannot spend it |
| Event Horizon | 0.20 / 0.30 / 0.40 / 0.50 m | Fields and mapped Catalyst area; requires an actual area producer |
| Crushing Pressure | 2 / 3 / 4 / 5 damage | Native compression/collapse against 600 ms continuous field exposure; requires a real field path |
| Rift Shear | 3 / 5 / 7 / 9 damage each 600 ms | Genuine intersection of at least two active fields; one strongest eligible owner, ties by peer ID |
| Singularity | Unique: 8 m range, 3 m radius, 1.5 s gather, 30 damage, 10 s cooldown | Additional targeted action; self-placement and distant placement work for every class |

Values remain provisional in `affinity_card_catalog.gd`. Singularity's gathering step/total are independently tunable at 0.22/1.10 m; Event Horizon and acquired sustained compression can improve it. Core-only damage/strength cards do not silently alter its separate tuning. A resistant boss can resist all movement and still take valid implosion/shear damage. Multiple fields are queried in linear enemy-by-field work, with one damage interval per enemy rather than an explosion for every field pair. Field identities, current life/connection/epoch and delayed enemy spawn identities are validated; secondary damage creates no additional fields or Catalyst/graft responses.

`card_active_actions.gd` extends its existing reliable request with a validated target distance for Singularity only; older six-field card requests are unchanged. B/Q/R, passive transformations, acquisition ownership, protocol-41 compact snapshots and RPC NodePaths remain intact. Existing configurable card-action bindings/skill-bar feedback are reused. Third/fourth active-action keys retain their existing user-configurable unbound defaults; no new control system is introduced.

Void verification passes eight focused suites / thirteen unit-peer verdicts: native Void, new control/overlap/implosion cases, CE01 eligibility, original EC01, effect-timeline ownership/rollback and guarded transactions, plus independent two-/four-player loops with duplicate Core/Singularity requests, live overlapping fields, field retirement, normal ledger/floor/extraction/rejoin flow and fifth-client refusal. Evidence: `.godot/validation/final-four-ce04-void-network-20261120-224535/summary.json`. Tests use disposable save storage. Local loopback is not separate-PC/internet verification; production AC-06 remains disabled.


### CE04 final eligibility, replication and verification boundaries

The completed gameplay catalog contains **137 cards / 12 Unique acquisitions**: all 101 CE03 definitions and their full numerical/eligibility data remain unchanged, with 10 Storm, 15 Spirit and 11 Void additions. The provisional 12-acquisition budget, four-copy numerical limits, independent rarity contributions, 2/4/6 Essence rerolls and six-graft playtest budget are unchanged. New tunables remain beside their displayed names in `affinity_card_catalog.gd`; ordinary dash recharge is `player_cooldowns.gd::BASELINE_DODGE_MS`.

- `affinity_card_ledger.gd` remains the sole offer/acquisition/headroom owner. New fixed-tempo cards declare that timing policy in their tuning. Their useful limit derives from the actual native weapon interval, owned Light Grip contributions, integer rounding and the existing 230 ms weapon floor. A last useful partial contribution can be offered; a further ineffective copy cannot. No new universal power ceiling is introduced.
- Grounded Current and Conductive Reach require a working native chain. A recipient-only Storm Catalyst requires acquired Relay Pulse first. Supporting identity alone never creates an electrical response. `void_affinity.gd::class_pull_supported` exposes the same unopposed-compression conditions used by actual displacement; Grasping Invocation is excluded when native class displacement always suppresses it. Dense Casting remains useful in those builds.
- `spirit_replicas.gd` owns the executable Wraithcast compatibility query. A weaker duplicate Ward or strongest-source Vital capacity alone cannot qualify a solo build. Useful class damage/opening-healing copies or Chain Spark/Ironbark can qualify it; supplemental Ward/Vital copies remain supported for an otherwise qualified owner. Movement, traps, persistent fields, native learned-node callbacks, original cooldown/resource costs, Catalyst and graft procs are not replayed. Siphon Cut copies damage only, not self-drain or party healing; its reach uses `class_skill_balance.gd::range_limit`, the same authoritative calculation as ordinary class targeting.
- Wraithcast Chain Spark retains captured jump/reach investment, Grounded Current and Conductor's finite forks/return at reduced damage. Its leaf execution cannot generate new shadows or echoes. Mirrorstep reproduces all 15 native class weapon patterns through the existing weapon queue, using a fixed departure position and one native projectile muzzle offset. Water leaf copies contribute scaled native Saturation/Drenched without advancing ordinary Rolling Current or Refreshing Strikes counters. Neither phantom claims native ordinary follow-up or Harvest spending.
- Native Ready Footing/Veil restoration preserves the existing restoration timestamp while restoring one sequential charge. HUD passive counts are separated from additional active slots; Stored Momentum is labeled in dash charges. Reliable owner details carry charge/passive counts; protocol 41's fast layout and RPC signatures remain unchanged.

The final multi-system integration run at `.godot/validation/final-four-ce04-final-20261120-231601/summary.json` passed **40 selected suites / 61 unit-peer verdicts**, covering all nine affinity baselines, all five classes, original cards, eligibility/rarity/headroom, effect/weapon ownership, terrain, transactions and network compatibility. After the final damage-only Siphon refinement, **nine affected current-source suites / fourteen verdicts** passed at `.godot/validation/final-four-ce04-siphon-safe-20261120-232211/summary.json`; this includes independent Spirit two-/four-player runs, Reaper/Oracle, native class actions, eligibility, RPC contract and guarded transactions. Original logs and failed attempts remain intact. The preceding wider keys are historical after that narrow correction; no stale result was relabeled as current.

Storm, Spirit and Void each passed independent host/guest and host/three-guest local loopback, including fifth-client refusal, actual card-enhanced combat, normal card/graft transactions, floor continuity, extraction and Garden rejoin. Matching-build admission/action readiness and definitions-mismatch refusal passed; mismatched guests are refused before authenticated admission. Every runner uses disposable per-peer `.godot` save storage. All **106 validation/deployment-tool self-tests** passed (`final-four-step1-tools-20261120-232459`). The final synthetic Spirit stress case keeps 128 echoes plus 128 shadows across four players, creating no additional actor instances: approximately 35.2 ms total preparation, 9.5 ms queue admission and 7.0 ms contact resolution on this machine. These timings are not separate-PC, internet, GPU or manual frame-rate evidence.

The shared definition/integration graph conservatively affects **270 registered suites**. This milestone refreshes focused evidence only; broader source/package release evidence remains pending. No main merge, remote push, export, protected-tag change, AI/terrain edit or production save activation occurred. `playtest.5`, the CE01 branch and retained design-reference stashes remain unchanged.

Unfinished controls and validation remain explicit:

- Settings currently stores keyboard/mouse bindings only. Controller rebinding for additional acquired actions is **not implemented**; it requires extending `app_settings.gd` serialization/event conversion and `settings_panel.gd` binding capture. Existing partial joypad handling in native aiming does not supply those acquired-action bindings. Slots three/four retain their existing unbound defaults and can be assigned keyboard/mouse controls in Settings.
- Wraithcast supports the documented portions, not every skill: six other Core families, traps and persistent fields are unsupported and retain shadows. Ironbark copies protection; Vital Bloom copies capacity/regeneration. Activation-triggered card volleys and learned-node follow-ups are not repeated.
- The pre-existing `guarded_lifecycle` preferences failure is still unresolved: old custom B-bound attacks can leave Core without a migrated shortcut because CE02 already assigns X/Z. The fixture and settings implementation were not changed by CE04. Its retained failure is separate from the passing guarded gameplay transactions; AC-06 deployment/recovery is not claimed closed.
- Production persistence remains disabled and host-memory crash-loss limitations remain. Manual visuals, combat feel, controller integration and separate-PC/internet co-op remain unverified. No further card family is started by this checkpoint.

## CE04 Windows integration repairs

Local `main` now includes CE01–CE04 by fast-forward from `e1265e0dd750c4b1d63382921d574ccf11fd0562` to `2ca533599a2032119bc881de210c68e717cddaa5`. The recovery tag is `recovery/main-before-ce04-playtest-20261120-234251`. Subsequent release repairs preserve the 137-card catalog, balance values, protocol 41 and the production persistence gate.

- `app_settings.gd::normalize` reserves existing custom controls before assigning new card defaults. An old B-bound attack can retain its migrated Core shortcut; fresh B/Z/X defaults are unchanged.
- `expedition_pressure.gd::plan` tries the original room sites first, then certified navigation positions when room centers are reserved for the leader or exit. Existing safe distances, population limits, warning duration, reward exclusions and isolated RNG remain authoritative.
- `assets/terrain_m02/experimental/experiment_garden.gd` accepts successful normal transactions as well as typed committed guarded outcomes for offerings, expansion and construction. Failed and uncertain outcomes are still refused. This does not enable the guarded production cutover.
- `root_ui.gd::_notification` frees prepared HUD nodes if a scene is discarded before `_ready` can parent them. Normal mounted children retain ordinary scene ownership.

Current tests retain real reward/leave and aimed-class RPC assertions. Their setup now supplies reward baselines, matching floor identities, explicit cover, a complete renderable mock layout, and a legal construction position. Research unlocks construction capacity; fixtures that need a bed or station place it through the current validator and transaction. The old circular Garden/scenery assertions are retired; modern Garden expansion, construction and water fixtures own those contracts. Health, appearance, historical progression conversion, rollback and save-isolation checks remain.

The maintained release pipeline in `tools/playtest_release.py` builds a clean committed checkout and verifies source, packaged and retail evidence before publishing. A numbered retail playtest enables persistent saving in its own `%APPDATA%/Mystical Garden Playtest <version>` directory, separate from prototype and previous-version saves. `PRODUCTION_CUTOVER_APPROVED` remains false; AC-06 deployment acceptance, host-crash recovery, controller rebinding, and separate-PC/internet verification remain separate concerns. The archived build manifest and evidence record the actual final verification results.

## Character customization on the current models

`character_appearance.gd` is the authoritative definition for labels, choices, tooltips, defaults, saved-field mapping and optional mesh names. The creator and Garden appearance menu use the same definitions. Clothing and hood/armor controls are labelled as tints because they multiply existing textured colours; material values and textures are unchanged. Hairstyles are Parted, Buns and Long. The hood is independent: it conceals hair while worn, then restores the selected style. Optional shoulder armor, bracers and both extra waist belts can be hidden independently. The fixed belt in the clothing texture remains. Main clothing, arms, legs and footwear stay attached; the exports do not contain full replacement body geometry beneath those pieces.

| Editing task | Authoritative location |
|---|---|
| Labels, palette names, defaults and accessory mapping | `character_appearance.gd`: `LABELS`, `OPTIONS`, `TOOLTIPS`, `OPTION_FIELDS`, `MESH_OPTIONS`, `normalize`, `cycle_option`, `apply`. |
| Creator controls and live preview | `character_creator.gd`; all seven existing height/build/shoulder/face sliders retain their original fields and ranges. |
| Garden appearance controls | `ui/gameplay/gameplay_ui.tscn` authors the buttons; its controller presents normalized values and emits option indices. `Game._garden_action` retains host authorization, expedition locking and existing transaction coordination. |
| Visible models/accessories | `assets/characters/field_traveller_male.glb` and `field_traveller_female.glb`; assembly remains in `tools/build_field_travellers.py`. Keep the 65-joint skeleton, existing mesh names and supported morph channels. Each actor owns one visible skeleton. Class and Core casts use the two animation-only field_skill_poses libraries. See [Character models](CHARACTER_MODELS.md). |
| Compact projection and compatibility | `world_snapshot.gd::APPEARANCE_BITS`; `network_contract.gd` includes appearance capability version 2. |

The original 13 appearance fields keep their meanings. Fields 13–16 append hood, shoulder armor, bracers and extra belts. Old arrays retain their body, colours and proportions; the old hairstyle-zero hood rule initializes the new hood field once, with accessories enabled. Normalization returns detached values. Character identity, XP and other progression remain in their existing profile owner; no new customization ledger or save writer was added. The four flags use the previously unused upper four bits of the existing 64-bit appearance field. Protocol 41, packet size, player-record size, RPC arguments and NodePaths remain unchanged. Older builds fail the existing compatibility handshake rather than silently interpreting the new choices incorrectly.

`tests/character_customization.gd` covers legacy normalization, all 16 flag combinations through the codec, 96 body/style/accessory combinations, preserved body coverage and both interfaces. `character_customization_coop` covers actual registration, host-authorized changes, public/private agreement, invalid requests, expedition locking and saved guest cosmetics. Existing rig, proportion, locomotion, weapon, roster, UI, codec and guarded-profile tests remain. Source-only changes require restarting a running scene; exported builds are updated separately.

### Weapon carry and attack presentation

`weapon_pose_catalog.gd` is the editable presentation definition for all fifteen weapons: carry angles, model-axis corrections, source clips, normalized visual impact markers, relaxed-carry policy, upper-body stance and utility hand paths. Angles are degrees and offsets are metres. Combat release times, cooldowns, damage, reach and collision remain in `assets/characters/weapon_actions.json` and the existing combat owners; no combat values changed for these corrections.

`traveller_weapon_animation.gd` retargets nine selected upper-body clips from the two free Quaternius Standard libraries onto the visible 65-joint rig. It owns no Game, action clock or gameplay state. `traveller_locomotion.gd::sample_clip` reads animation tracks into detached pose values, starting unkeyed joints from rest or the explicitly supplied locomotion layer. It never treats the previously posed skeleton as a default. This prevents omitted constant tracks from retaining a previous attack or accumulating a stance. Playback names/positions remain available for presentation inspection, without using AnimationPlayer to overwrite the rig during sampling. Existing idle/walk/run curves and planted/flight phases are retained.

`field_traveller.gd` is the sole character pose writer. It blends locomotion and attacks, then solves grip constraints from a fresh input pose. Previously solved arm rotations must not be fed into the next frame's grip input: that accumulates wrist twist. The two-bone solve aligns both the segment direction and elbow plane, preserving bone translations, scale and reach. Class casts retain their existing authored driver. Affected one-handed weapons and the resting bow/scythe use natural idle/walk arms rather than a permanent two-handed guard. Their constraints fade in/out with the existing attack phase.

The spear uses the two reviewed Blender actions through `spear_animation.gd`: a side-held thrust followed by the rear-grip frontal sweep. Its imported upper-body rotations and baked grip tracks replace the former procedural basic stance/path. The bow turns the upper body side-on and draws along an elevated, lateral plane outside the chest; its resting draw arm is free. The scythe's opposite axial correction points the blade down, with a relaxed carry and independently authored sweep. Ring carry hangs below the hand at the side. Bell/censer/fan hand paths and recovery stay forward of the body, and the fan uses a lateral source gesture. `weapon_visuals.gd` shares these orientation definitions and derived support-grip markers. Staff/wand and Seed Focus retain their existing release/muzzle alignment.

The clip asset and reproducible extraction are `assets/characters/weapon_combat.glb`, `weapon_combat_sources.json` and `tools/build_weapon_combat_library.py`; provenance/licensing is in `assets/source/ART_CREDITS.md`. Source archives are not runtime dependencies. Rig replacement must preserve named joints; weapon replacement must preserve grip/tip coordinates in `plant_weapon_models.gd` and `plant_polearms.gd`.

`weapon_presentation` covers both bodies, carry/attack/recovery, omitted-track independence, relaxed arms, conservative torso-capsule clearance, level forward thrust, downward scythe blade, ring height, nock alignment, source clip masking, continuous joints and unchanged release markers. The torso capsule detects gross arm intersections; native visual review remains necessary for exact mesh contact and subjective motion quality. `character_overhaul` retains 90 body/proportion/weapon combinations and moving grip checks. `character_locomotion`, `weapon_actions`, `combat_animation` and `projectile_terrain` retain gait, timing, muzzle and collision contracts. `weapon_actions_coop` checks the replicated bow, elevated focus and scythe grip; `four_players_coop` retains independent local party/admission coverage.

The original `9014b07` checkpoint's 17 suites/23 verdicts checked grips but missed retained unkeyed joints, rigid carry and forearm/body intersections. Those gaps now have explicit regressions. Native full-cycle captures use `Stage.update_avatar`, frontal/diagonal facing and the gameplay camera angle for twelve weapons on both bodies, including complete attacks, idle recovery and walking. The earlier failed checks remain as historical logs. Main, installed playtests, protected tags, real saves, production cutover settings, design-reference stashes and the pre-existing card-formatting edit remain unchanged. Broad release evidence and separate-PC/internet testing are outside this correction. Final correction evidence: `.godot/validation/executions/weapon-body-verified/` contains **17 passing suites / 23 unit-peer verdicts**, including fifth-client refusal, guarded transactions and customization/proportion compatibility. All **106 tooling self-tests** pass. The six native full-cycle captures contain no engine/script errors or warnings. The focused preview still identifies 139 consumers out of 279 registered suites; no broad release evidence was refreshed.

### Alternating spear attacks

`assets/characters/spear_thrust.glb` and `spear_sweep.glb` contain the approved Blender actions. `spear_motion.json` contains their generated 24 FPS grip/orientation observations and contact times (thrust frame 22, sweep frame 41). The editable review originals remain in `Documents/Codex/Blender Tests/`: `Mannequin - Spear Thrust Review - 20261121-172356.blend` and `Mannequin - Spear Sweep Review - 20261121-180420.blend`. The latter retains both actions; neither original's action keys were changed during export. `spear_animation.gd` samples them and calibrates palm sockets; `field_traveller.gd::pose_spear_hands` fits the authored motion to the target arms. It does not author a second thrust/sweep trajectory. Keep the 65 named joints and the spear model's local +Y shaft/grip coordinates when replacing assets.

`assets/characters/weapon_actions.json::spear.basic_variants` is the authoritative attack order and footprint definition. The first basic is a narrow 0.14 m half-width thrust; the second is a 125-degree sweep, centred 7.5 degrees toward the finishing side (stored in radians). `PlayerWeaponActions.State` owns the basic-only step, independently of the event sequence; Core poses and refused starts cannot disturb alternation. `WeaponAttackWorld` freezes the selected clip into captured stats. `WeaponContact.describe` feeds both damage and the replicated footprint, preserving secondary chain/splash semantics. `weapon_visuals.gd` uses the same selected clip's sliding grip offsets. The existing 650 ms native interval, 420 ms pose, 176 ms release, resolved damage/reach and target budget remain unchanged; faster builds retain the existing timing limits.

Protocol 41 and its existing clip/delivery fields are unchanged. Reliable swing events include the sector's yaw offset. Weapon profiles now participate in the existing gameplay-definition handshake, so an older client with line-only spear rules cannot join this build. No new authority, actor registry or saved progression fields were added.

Focused verification (2026-11-21, Godot 4.7.2): `spear_attacks`, weapon action/queue/presentation, locomotion/proportions, projectile terrain, Spirit replicas, codec, network contract and guarded transactions pass. Independent two-player checks see thrust then sweep in snapshots, visible clips and swing events, with authoritative front/flank damage. The four-player control passes, including fifth-player refusal: **16 suites / 22 strict process verdicts**. The two bodies cover 1,936 imported poses with sub-micrometre grip error, 15.5/17.5 cm minimum conservative forearm clearance and zero lower-body difference from independent locomotion-only actors. Native rendered review also checks both clips and their distinct outlines. These results do not establish separate-PC/internet behavior or final subjective animation approval. Evidence is retained under `Documents/Codex/2026-11-05/referenced-chatgpt-conversation-this-is-an/work/spear_integration/verified/`. All 106 tooling checks pass across the main self-test run and the separately permitted disposable NTFS-link check. The targeted planner identifies 181 affected consumers among 280 registered suites with no scope warnings; broad release evidence is not refreshed. Real saves, main, protected tags and existing exports are unchanged.

### Authored Spore Staff casts and projectile launch

## Saved Garden development and movable starting structures

Pre-HG03 policy (2026-11-21): the generated terrain is the Garden's only water source. Existing Root Irrigation enables the same one-vessel filling at its reachable dry bank; crop watering and research requirements are unchanged. No separate pond/basin is generated.

| Editing location | Authority / contract |
|---|---|
| `garden_structure_catalog.gd` | One readable definition of names, art and footprint half-extents in metres. The starting Garden Altar is move-only; it cannot be purchased again. |
| `assets/terrain_m02/garden_construction.gd` | Live placement, stable structure IDs, collision, dry footprint and access validation. All owned beds, including the first three, and the starting altar use these records. Crop data remains in `Progress.plots`; moving never buys another crop slot. |
| `assets/terrain_m02/experimental/experiment_garden.gd` | Host construction/expansion coordination, Garden Development UI and detached save/network projections. The move list names Starting bed 1–3 and Garden Altar. `bed_position` / `station_position` feed rendering and interaction; no fixed-position fallback remains for owned starting structures. |
| `garden_development_state.gd`, `progress.gd` | Version-1 optional `garden_development/state` ConfigFile section: terrain generation, outline/owned cells, expansion/root history, stable placements, growth energy and pending Heart studies. Missing old-save data adopts already owned beds and the altar without charging again. Malformed/unsupported saved data refuses loading and subsequent writes rather than silently replacing the layout. |
| `_commit_development` in the Garden integration | Prepare a data-only candidate, restore confirmed live owners, then save the candidate and associated crop/part/Knowledge changes through the existing transaction. Publish/apply only a confirmed commit, including committed cleanup-pending outcomes. Failed or uncertain saves retain confirmed live placement; existing session holds block unsafe replay. Save projections are detached values, not another active placement authority. |
| `stage.gd`, `garden_interaction_view.gd`, `garden_return_view.gd` | Read current bed/altar positions. Relationship roots refresh after layout changes; water discovery colors the terrain material. The retired decorative pond mesh/meta path has no live caller. |

Ordinary closing/reopening restores the host Garden layout with crop contents. Reclaimed land and structural roots persist together so buildings do not reload outside their clearing. Material contributions commit the shared inventory debit, Growth Energy and Knowledge together. There is no per-frame disk write or new standalone save file. Guest layouts remain reliable detached host projections; a guest cannot move buildings or save the host's Garden.

Garden compatibility advances from `modern-terrain-3` to `modern-terrain-4`, since older clients cannot represent a relocated starting altar. The existing handshake rejects unlike Garden contracts; RPC NodePaths and protocol-41 compact packet layout remain unchanged. Production AC-06 activation remains disabled, and host-crash recovery is still outside this feature.

Focused ownership: `garden_layout_persistence` covers real Title/New/Continue, all starting structures, planted crops, expansion/root/resource reload, study deduplication, rollback and malformed saves; `garden_layout_transactions` covers real guarded failure, cleanup-pending, uncertainty, replay blocking and recovery. Existing `experimental_terrain_coop`, water, growth, conduit, return, serializer and four-player controls cover their live integrations. Broader release evidence and export remain separate.

Verification (2026-11-21): `garden-layout-verified` passes 30 suites / 44 independent process verdicts (ten two-player pairs and the four-player/overflow control). Matching builds pass action readiness; changed definitions refuse admission. All 74 validation-tool self-tests pass when run after multiplayer releases its fixture lock. A new rendered process restores a copy of the disposable saved Garden, including stable bed/altar positions and reclaimed root history; the menu and actual shoreline were visually inspected. Earlier restricted connection attempts and the incomplete display-stage fixture remain recorded, not relabeled as passes. Twelve normalized base source identities are preserved by narrow Garden scopes; the Garden/network preview affects 88 of 273 registered suites. This is focused verification, not a package or broad release refresh. Protected tags, six design references, main and the existing playtest remain intact.

Saving layouts does not change launcher policy: an ordinary persistent Garden restores this data, while the existing deliberately disposable source launcher still creates a fresh isolated Garden each launch. The existing numbered EXE is not rebuilt by this source checkpoint.

Spring construction reservations are explicit content flags: `garden_interaction_catalog.gd` marks `spring_water` and `spring_weave` with `blocks_construction: false`. Their historical IDs, observation conditions and scenery exclusions remain intact; neither fixed marker reserves an empty building site. `assets/terrain_m02/experimental/experiment_garden.gd` carries the flag to the generated landmark and `garden_construction.gd::placement_issue` honors it (other landmarks default to protected). Actual terrain water, the mature Heart base, existing structures and access remain protected. Keeping the generation reservations intact avoids changing saved terrain/root replay. The layout schema is unchanged. Verification (2026-11-21): 11 focused suites / 13 passing process verdicts in disposable saves cover the former water site at (6.0, 1.5), actual nearby bed overlap, relocation/save/reload and host/guest placement; water filling and the existing observation remain available. The original refusal and two corrected fixture-setup failures remain recorded separately. No production writer, protocol, main branch or exported build changed. Restart a running scene to load the new reservation flags.

## Walking collision and fitted footprints

Garden and Wilds use `assets/terrain_m02/terrain_actor_motion.gd` for ground-plane walking. A sweep reaches the first legal contact, then projects the remaining movement along the contacted edge. There is no X/Z fallback, speed boost or automatic choice of a side: a glancing contact slides, a direct head-on contact stops. Existing owner legality queries still govern water, decks, terrain boundaries and solid obstacles. Straight dash sweeps do not steer around obstacles.

| Change | Authoritative location and units |
|---|---|
| Walking and contact resolution | `terrain_actor_motion.gd`: existing radius 0.34 m, 0.13 m sampling, 0.001 m contact gap; at most five contacts per step bounds work in closed corners. |
| Rock/tree mesh, sizing and placement | `terrain_prop_geometry.gd` shares the existing procedural recipes/transforms with `terrain_feature_view.gd`. Walking outlines are cached convex projections; tree collision samples 10–30% of mesh height, excluding canopy and low roots. |
| Step-over stones | `terrain_prop_geometry.gd::STEP_OVER_HEIGHT_M`, currently 0.18 m of actual placed visual height. Applies only to terrain rocks; structures and explicit authored walls remain solid. Unknown/authored collision shapes retain their supplied geometry. |
| Garden walking bodies | `garden_forest_barrier.gd` derives prop bodies on bind. `garden_construction.gd::walking_footprint` derives station outlines from `garden_station_art.gd`, excluding decorative bed shoots. Mesh projection is shared in `terrain_feature_geometry.gd`. |
| Wilds walking/cover | `wilds_play_surface.gd` uses fitted bodies for movement. TC01 projectile/sight cover retains its original authored rocks, trunk disks and dry perimeter strips, including the low-rubble distinction. |

These caches contain derived geometry only. Construction placement/door reservations, reclamation occupancy, saved terrain replay, structure records and crop ownership remain unchanged. The Heart's mature ground-base construction exclusion is unchanged. All body projections happen on setup/placement, not every movement frame. Host movement and guest prediction call the same Garden/Wilds operations. `network_contract.gd` adds movement capability version 1 so incompatible movement builds cannot join; protocol 41, the 25 RPC signatures, packet layouts, terrain version and save schemas remain unchanged.

Verification (2026-11-21, Godot 4.7.2): **27 focused suites / 36 strict process verdicts** passed. Five affected terrain suites were rechecked after preserving dry perimeter cover, with all six process verdicts passing. `tests/terrain_actor_collision.gd` covers round/angled contacts, symmetry, head-on stops, 60/240 Hz steps, closed corners, bank/deck edges, step-over stones, actual mesh footprints/transforms and detached caches. The collision-only four-actor benchmark took approximately 19 ms for 240 steps with 41 obstacles; this is not a whole-game FPS result. Independent terrain co-op compares host/guest sliding at relocated beds and checks saved layout publication, floor travel, extraction and rejoin. Movement prediction/reconciliation, matching-build action readiness, mismatch refusal with zero admissions, four players and fifth-player refusal pass. Related checks cover construction/save rollback, Heart restrictions, Spirit/Void movement, projectile clearance, boss layouts, a 60-layout route corpus and solo startup. The isolated startup debugger capture has zero warning/error records; all 106 validation-tool self-tests pass.

The coverage registry now has 276 suites (one added), with no unresolved ownership/scope warnings. The initial connected preview selected 266 of the previous 275 suites with zero reusable results; broad existing release evidence is not refreshed by this focused pass. No export, real-save writes, protected-history changes or main promotion were performed. Restart a running scene to rebuild its derived collision caches. Local automated tests do not establish separate-PC/internet behavior or replace manual review of movement feel.

## Heart base footprint and startup diagnostics

The clarified construction exclusion follows the **ground-level trunk/root base at maximum growth**, leaving ground beneath overhead branches buildable. `assets/terrain_m02/terrain_heart_preview.gd::maximum_base_outline` derives a convex base boundary from the final imported mesh at the mature reservation scale. The shared `terrain_feature_geometry.gd::project_height_band` includes exposed geometry between ground level and `BUILD_BASE_HEIGHT_M` (0.35 metres), including triangle-edge crossings so long faces cannot escape the restriction. Wholly buried or overhead geometry is excluded. The outline is cached once and returned as detached copies; cursor previews do not rescan the mesh. `research_scale` remains the shared width/height calculation. HG01 preserves the pre-existing 22-upgrade minimum scale for this outline so retiring Research Infusion leaves placement permissions intact. There is no separate circular build radius.

`assets/terrain_m02/garden_construction.gd` substitutes that outline only for the Heart's placement reservation and identifies the blocker as the fully grown Heart's base. The old circular scenery clearing, canopy and decorative reclamation roots add no independent construction barrier. Water, occupied trunks/rocks, other structures, research and access checks remain enforced. Existing starter/saved structures and crops are retained; newly built or relocated structures must fit outside the base. Terrain generation, expansion ownership, root rendering and saved root history are unchanged.

Startup investigation reproduced **213 GDScript warnings across 94 scripts**, rather than 213 runtime failures. Scoped declaration names now avoid shadowing; unused values are removed, nullable/enum handling is explicit, and compatible ternaries retain their actual types. Existing integer calculations are unchanged: bounded, commented warning annotations document deliberate truncation in time, fixed-point damage, historical XP and grid indexing. The externally emitted outgoing-contact signal remains available to the Neural Arena. Project warning settings and enemy decision interfaces are unchanged. `app_settings.gd::apply` lets the editor control embedded window mode, preserving the saved fullscreen preference for standalone play.

All three starter beds and the altar move before research; a newly researched fourth bed fits beneath the canopy without reclamation or clearing research. `tests/support/garden_placement_case.gd` locates legal fixture placements through the real validator, avoiding fixed art coordinates. Layout, research and co-op fixtures preserve their existing economy, identity and transaction assertions.

### Vine Bow, Bark Arbalest, Thorn Ring and Graft Knives

The visible travellers alternate the upright and canted bow shots, raised and low arbalest shots, sidearm and rising ring throws, and right crosscut and left rising knife cut. Each pair has a shared ready pose and two authored recoveries. The host chooses the clip using the accepted basic attack count in `assets/characters/weapon_actions.json`. Contact uses the existing weapon timing and delivery, including the crafted knife line.

`reviewed_weapon_attacks.gd` samples the upper-body pose and matching prop placement. `field_traveller.gd` fits the support hand to each traveller’s reach. `weapon_visuals.gd` draws the held props, flexing bow limbs, strings and nocked ammunition. The enlarged bow uses 1.5 times the standard held-mesh scale. Arrows, bolts and thrown rings start at the reviewed release point and then follow the combat projectile.

The four Blender filenames and source hashes are in `assets/characters/ranger_and_knife_grips.json`. Run `tools/export_reviewed_weapon_attacks.py` in Blender to regenerate the grip samples, the 20 clips in `assets/characters/ranger_and_knife_attacks.glb` and the bow’s Draw shape. Geometry and timing checks are in `tests/reviewed_weapon_attacks.gd`. The independent host/guest checks are in `tests/weapon_actions_coop.gd`.

Bloom Bell alternates two lantern lifts, Spore Censer alternates forward and diagonal swings with a skinned suspension, and Petal Fan alternates forehand and left-coil backhand volleys. Each stroke has its own return to ready. The authored Blender scenes are named in `assets/characters/{bell,censer,fan}_grips.json`. Rebuild a pair with `tools/export_oracle_attacks.py` and select the basic clips in `assets/characters/weapon_actions.json`. Fan visuals start at the three sampled rim positions, using the original cast direction for the whole volley. The host still resolves the existing projectile paths and shared hit budget.

Harvest Scythe alternates two overhand sweeps with separate recoveries. The hooked edge crosses on the first stroke and reverses on the return. Both contact at 48% of the basic attack. The `scythe.basic_variants` entries in `assets/characters/weapon_actions.json` define the two ground sectors used by the indicator and host collision. The crafted line uses its own attack. To revise the animation or hand placement, open the Blender source named in `assets/characters/scythe_grips.json` and run `tools/export_scythe.py`.

Root Lash alternates a forehand flick and a backhand that gathers a loop on the traveller’s left and extends forward. Both contact at 46% of the attack. The `whip` entry in `assets/characters/weapon_actions.json` defines their ground paths as lateral metres and fractions of resolved reach. `weapon_contact.gd` uses those paths for the hit indicator and host collision. The crafted sweep keeps its separate sector. To revise the motion, open the Blender review named in `assets/characters/root_lash_grips.json` and run `tools/export_root_lash.py` to regenerate the five body clips and the matching 18-link poses. `tests/weapon_actions.gd` checks both travellers against the indicator at base and increased reach.
