| Change | Authoritative editing path |
|---|---|
| Research identity, benefit, prerequisites and price | `heart_catalog.gd` defines each node. `insight` is the spendable price. Lifetime Knowledge tracks earnings. The starting Living Archive displays guardian records. |
| Purchase | `garden_commands_world.gd` admits the host at the Heart between expeditions. `heart_progress.gd::purchase` checks ownership, prerequisites and spendable Knowledge, records spending and immediately applies the unlock in the existing Garden transaction. Seed propagation is a starting capability. Bed and station permissions take effect in Build. |
| Material contributions | `assets/terrain_m02/garden_offerings.gd` prices each available shared plant or monster part. In Common through Mythic order the per-unit Growth Energy values are 2/3/4/6/9 and Knowledge values are 1/2/3/5/8. `experimental/experiment_garden.gd::contribute` rechecks the quote and reservations and commits both rewards and the debit together. A committed contribution advances the saved Garden revision. |
| Persistence | Heart v4 retains owned IDs, total Knowledge spent and Field Study trophy history. Layout v3 holds terrain, roots and Energy alongside the construction inventory. On load, supported v1/v2 paid research becomes owned. Recorded Infusion spending is refundable up to five, limited to the original debit above supported purchases. A free grant carries no refund. Each outstanding layout-v1 batch pays its entire remaining Knowledge entitlement. Its Energy was already paid. The next ordinary save backs up the original file and commits both projections together. |
| Presentation and verification | `progression_panel.gd` shows price, prerequisites, available Knowledge and benefit. `garden_offerings_view.gd` previews both currencies. Reliable Garden details publish confirmed unlocks and currency balances. The session handshake requires immediate-Heart support. Compact protocol 41 slots 0 and 1 of the Garden array remain reserved. See `tests/heart_foundation.gd`, `guarded_garden_actions.gd`, `serializer_integrity.gd`, and the research and wild flora multiplayer fixtures. |

Crop growth, watering and Patient Bud keep their cultivation clocks. Research uses no simulation ticks or expedition-end bonuses.


## Garden construction (HG02)

The host opens **Build** beside the Heart. **Inventory** deploys an owned structure, **Construct** pays for a new stored instance, and **Placed** relocates or stores an instance. Permanent deconstruction requires confirmation displaying the original payment. Guests can inspect shared development. The placement preview checks footprints, occupants, dry ownership, the Heart base, scenery, and walking access to doors, water and the gate.

| Change | Authoritative editing path |
|---|---|
| Types and costs | `garden_structure_catalog.gd` defines footprint half-extents in metres, art, unlock, repeatability and a payment in Growth Energy and shared part IDs. Cultivation Grounds costs 4 Knowledge and grants one bed. Additional beds cost 12 Growth Energy; HG03 adds Living Sprinklers at 24. The Seed Cache and Field Study each grant one unique instance. |
| Ownership | `progress.gd::structures` holds the shared inventory. `garden_structure_inventory.gd` allocates monotonically increasing instance IDs and validates the saved records. Each record has its pose, placement state, crop slot and original payment. Storing a structure releases its footprint and service. An occupied bed can move with its entire crop record. Harvest before storing or dismantling it. |
| Commands and durability | `assets/terrain_m02/experimental/experiment_garden.gd` validates host operations and reviewed revisions, then commits the debit, grant or refund and ownership together through the existing Garden transaction. A removed ID cannot be reused. Free structures have an empty receipt and can be stored or redeployed. Research and its first structure grant share the Heart purchase transaction. |
| Cultivation and rendering | `progress.gd::plots` remains the crop owner. A previously deployed bed keeps its crop slot while stored or relocated. An empty retired slot cannot be planted. `stage.gd::update_plots` reuses unaffected bed models. |
| Land and navigation | `garden_expansion.gd` owns the clearing and frontier proposals. Fresh Gardens have a 0.85 m starting margin. The larger substrate preserves the original inner habitat. `garden_construction.gd` checks physical overlap and connected walking approaches when previewing or committing. The existing frontier price uses one Energy per usable cell plus one per eight previously reclaimed cells, then applies the tier discount and rounds up. |
| Save migration | Garden layout v3 and construction inventory v1 share one save. HG01 placements retain their ID, position, orientation and crop. Each unused distinct numbered permit supplies one stored bed after counting existing additional beds. Existing permit spending stays spent and grants Cultivation Grounds. Unplaced researched stations receive one copy. Existing free instances receive no payment receipt. The next normal save backs up the pre-construction file. |
| Multiplayer | The existing reliable Garden details carry versioned ownership and placement. Hosts and guests must support the same construction version (HG02 used `structure_inventory: 1`, HG03 uses `2`) during the compatibility handshake. Compact crop data remains three bytes per allocated slot. Combat snapshot fields keep their protocol-41 layout. |

## Living Garden infrastructure (HG03)

| Responsibility | Authoritative editing path |
|---|---|
| Unlock and purchase | Living Sprinklers retains the `irrigation` identity. Reaching Roots and 4 spendable Knowledge unlock one free Water Intake and one free sprinkler. `heart_catalog.gd` defines the research and `garden_structure_catalog.gd` defines both structures, the 3.5 m radius, and the 24 Growth Energy repeat purchase. |
| Supply and placement | `garden_water.gd::intake_shore` finds an actual natural shoreline within 1.65 m. The HG02 validator also checks the entire dry footprint and access. One placed intake supplies every sprinkler. |
| Growth | `garden_irrigation.gd` rebuilds the crop-slot coverage index when structure ownership or positions change. It measures horizontal center distance with an inclusive boundary. `progress.gd::advance_growth` and `garden_cultivation_world.gd` use the same result for growth and inspection. `cultivation_growth.gd::WATER_RATE` sets a single 1.25 multiplier for manual water or active coverage. Players can fill their watering vessel at a reachable natural bank from the start. Manual care lasts until harvest; sprinkler coverage follows the live layout. HG04 moves Nursery familiarity into local coverage and gives growth acceleration to Vital Roots. Expedition growth still doubles. |
| Appearance and Build | `garden_infrastructure_view.gd` uses the frontier planner and mesh in `assets/terrain_m02/garden_heart_roots.gd`. Structure roots emerge from the sculpted Heart’s six buttresses or branch from an existing limb, follow dry ground and dip beneath water and structure footprints. Frontier, structure and Heart bark use `heart_bark.gdshader`. Placing or moving a structure reveals its new branch over 0.85 seconds. Loaded connections appear fully grown. The intake reaches the actual shore. Roots share one mesh. Spray, inlet ripples and pollen each share one nonblocking mesh per Garden. The synchronized irrigation owner supplies the active-water flag. The Build preview rings use the coverage index, and Placed lists supply and covered bed numbers. The separate frontier-growth roots still drive expansion cinematics. |
| Compatibility and recovery | HG02 inventory v1 upgrades to v2 on the existing Garden save path. A prior irrigation purchase grants both free structures into storage once, preserving manual watering, receipts and balances. The first normal migration save retains a pre-irrigation backup. Earlier layouts receive the same grants through their existing conversion. Buying, refunding and unlocking use the existing transactions. Reliable Garden details carry the placed instances and crop inspector facts. Each peer derives the root mesh locally. Combat packets retain their protocol-41 layout. |

## Local cultivation structures (HG04)

- **Catalog and ownership.** Edit structure prices, material IDs, radii and strengths in `garden_structure_catalog.gd`, and research prices and independent prerequisites in `heart_catalog.gd`. The HG02 construction inventory grants each initial instance and retains original payment receipts. A sanctuary keeps its disabled, standard or enhanced setting on its instance. Every new type participates in the existing Heart root mesh.
- **Coverage and time.** `garden_irrigation.gd::resolve` derives a per-bed effect map when the inventory revision or research changes. It uses inclusive horizontal center distances and the greatest eligible strength per effect. Automatic water requires a placed intake. `game.gd::apply_cultivation_change` settles outstanding growth at the old rate before changing infrastructure. `progress.gd::advance_growth` consumes the resolved rates and accepts configured care when work crosses the bud stage, including during a long update.
- **Harvest.** `garden_cultivation_world.gd` validates the host action and actor, and `garden_cultivation.gd` resolves the yield using pre-harvest familiarity and current coverage. `garden_crop_state.gd` owns the crop’s hidden draw, cycle, accepted care and manual watering state. The observer receives probabilities and effective gains. `altar_service.gd` applies Seed Conservation within the ordinary failed offering transaction. [Cultivation values and probabilities](SEED_PROGRESSION.md#local-cultivation-tuning-hg04)
- **Save conversion.** Heart v5 preserves existing research spending. Historical Nursery owners receive a Nursery and Vital Roots with their research. Historical Conduit owners receive a Bud Sanctuary and its effectiveness research. Flowering cost zero and retires without a refund. Migration reconciles missing free instances once, preserves prior irrigation grants and accepted crop care, and removes obsolete conduit settings. The existing save and recovery owners retain a pre-cultivation backup on conversion. Additional structures refund the Energy and materials recorded on their own receipt.
- **Build and multiplayer.** Build shows current research strength, radius, costs, actual coverage and disabled equipment. The existing Garden details carry placed structures, configuration, and research. The host supplies recipient-specific crop projections. Garden network capability `structure_inventory: 3` requires matching clients. Save inventory remains version 2. Derived coverage and root geometry are rebuilt from the layout.

## Wilds exploration research (HG05)

| Editing task | Authority and behavior |
|---|---|
| Research and balance | `heart_catalog.gd` defines the eight purchases. `wild_flora.gd` owns the gathering probabilities, awareness range and root appearance rates. Historical `checkpoint_deposit` ownership unlocks Returning Root immediately. Existing secured inventory needs no conversion. |
| Floor generation | After the actual encounter formation and WF01 specimen are known, `Game.start_encounter` asks `Battlefield.generate_returning_root` for at most one position. It uses a separate floor-seeded generator and the existing certified room-margin/trail-shoulder search. Search excludes enemy starts, water, obstructions and nearby interactables, including the specimen. A failed placement gives no root. Practice maps and authored arenas have none. |
| Harvest | `WildFlora.collect` records one party-wide claim only after bundle admission to the successful character. `InventoryModel.admit_wild_harvest` validates every destination before mutation and resolves ordinary/refined item IDs from the botanical catalog. Each bonus draw hashes a separate reward label with the specimen identity and stable character identity. Named seed banking or mystery seed identification remains the discovery boundary. |
| Local discovery | The host projects eligibility and shared discovery through reliable owner details. `Stage.show_wild_flora` enables a small local light only within the awareness radius and with terrain sight clearance. The HUD gives one five-second unfamiliar-life hint per floor. It discloses no direction, species or rarity. |
| Deposit | Interact within 1.55 m and clear sight of the physical root. `returning_root.gd` validates the current binding, life, run, floor, reviewed allowance and carried unit. `InventoryModel.take/put` transfers one unit to that character’s Garden stash, including exact ordinary or Ancient seed contents. The root accepts plant parts. Monster cargo can include pre-expedition supplies, so trophies are ineligible. |
| Transactions and replication | The same Garden transaction includes inventory, return credit and the per-character allowance in the floor savepoint. Verified rollback restores all three. Confirmed cleanup-pending retains the secured resource and spent allowance. Uncertain durability holds settlement for recovery. Reliable owner details carry one root and the recipient’s allowance. The stage builds a nonblocking Heart-bark mesh locally. Ongoing expeditions retain the existing reconnect policy. |

## Heart progression and the Garden economy (HG06B)

The starting Sheltered Heart owns three research branches. `heart_catalog.gd` orders 2 Garden Development, 20 Cultivation and 9 Wilds Exploration purchases, costing 7, 420 and 142 Knowledge respectively. `progression_panel.gd` shows branch lists, prerequisite branches, structure grants and benefits. The host validates every purchase at the Heart. Effectiveness and coverage each require only their structure research. For the cultivation and exploration values see [Seed progression](SEED_PROGRESSION.md).

`game.gd::_finish_combat` advances the expedition’s deepest cleared encounter. A standing participant can extract at a cleared six-encounter checkpoint. The party can consent together, or one explorer can leave individually during the checkpoint vote. `expedition_transitions.gd` validates the participant, life, binding, travel epoch, floor and gate distance. It calls `garden_return_service.gd::settle_extraction` inside the same settlement transaction as the loot. The existing Garden return record binds the run UUID to the participant and stores the highest cumulative Knowledge payout and the counts of guardians already returned. For B completed blocks, the payout is zero for B=0 and 4B−2 otherwise. Only the positive difference enters shared lifetime and spendable Knowledge. Pouch recovery, Returning Root deposits, wipes and abandonment award zero. Rollback restores both currency and watermark. Committed cleanup-pending keeps both, and uncertain durability holds further settlement until recovery. Reliable existing details carry the personal extraction action. Matching Heart research and travel-consent capability versions (both 2) are required.

The starting Living Archive displays decoded flags and campaign guardian counts. Field Study retains 4 Knowledge per first trophy and 1 per repeat and compares that reward with the same item's Heart contribution. Departure cargo admits eight trophy units. The cultivation terrace retains its observation. Fresh Gardens pay the existing undiscounted frontier formula throughout.

Heart save v6 keeps the actual spent balance. Importing v1/v2 deducts verified retired purchase costs from spending, limited to spending above other recorded purchases at their historical prices. Refunds leave lifetime earnings intact. Saves that retain only an aggregate debit preserve that debit. Previously earned 10% or 20% frontier discounts, prior decoded memories and the pre-conversion visual floor survive. Conversion uses the existing writer and captures the original bytes at the first save in the pre-heart-economy-v6 backup. Previously owned structures stay in their inventory. Existing recipe receipts and component-specific equipment craft receipts prevent retroactive first rewards.

Heart development counts one point per active research purchased, one per currently owned structure beyond the three initial beds and altar, and two per paid frontier. Storage and relocation preserve the count. Deconstruction removes the corresponding point. Stage thresholds 0, 4, 12, 24 and 40 select the five existing sculpts, ending at Living Sanctuary. Historical Gardens retain a minimum old stage and scale. Each point adds 1.8% height and 0.8% width through 22 points, preserving the mature ground-level reservation. The root mesh follows the placed layout. Land-purchase cutscenes follow the actual purchased frontier.

### HG06B pacing sensitivity

Rerunning the HG06A quarter-hour model with the implemented prices, rarity payments and undiscounted new frontiers gives the following counts of research purchases. Rows describe online Garden time. Fractional completion times express the model step size.

| Garden | 5 h | 15 h | 30 h | 50 h | 70 h | Completion |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Mixed solo | 5 | 13 | 19 | 27 | 31 | 55.75 h |
| Exploration solo | 6 | 14 | 19 | 25 | 31 | 64.75 h |
| Mixed duo | 6 | 15 | 22 | 31 | 31 | 42.25 h |
| Cultivation solo | 7 | 23 | 31 | 31 | 31 | 18.5 h |
| Optimized four | 10 | 30 | 31 | 31 | 31 | 15.25 h |

The model starts planting at 0.75 h, replaces seed stock through propagation, rounds maturity to the next visit, and covers up to four beds with the first specialized structures. In table order it assumes 1.5 / 0.65 / 1.5 / 5 / 3 Garden visits per hour, bed targets of 12 / 6 / 12 / 24 / 40, harvest capacities of 10 / 5 / 14 / 50 / 80 per hour, and donation shares of 45 / 30 / 45 / 65 / 65 percent. It assumes shared extraction income of 1 / 2.3 / 1 / 0.2 / 0.8 Knowledge per hour. For example, 0.2 six-clear runs, 0.15 twelve-clear runs and 0.1 eighteen-clear runs per hour average 2.3. These rates refer to distinct expedition payouts. The HG06A first-discovery/craft/study premium remains a pooled estimate, including recipe income. The model adds no second first-recipe income stream. Fresh Gardens pay undiscounted land prices even after three and six frontiers. Mixed and exploration solo still round to 56 and 65 hours. Manual timing of real extractions, Garden visits, and willingness to donate will determine how representative these figures are. Repeat trophy study and deliberate conversion of surplus propagated seeds merit particular attention.

### Developer Testing Arena

DeveloperMode authorizes the exact Developer Garden issued by the title flow. An editor or explicitly marked debug developer build and the solo developer session are both required. Settings exposes entry. The arena keeps the claimed Garden parked, renews its lease and creates a private viewport, multiplayer context, temporary character, Garden and combat session. Return destroys the practice world and resumes the original Garden. Suspended expeditions exclude arena entry. Practice uses ordinary combat and ER01 observations with in-memory owners, outside departure settlement and Archive aggregation. Developer expedition continuation stays in the Developer Garden save namespace.

Numbered Windows playtests may explicitly opt in to developer tools at export. Both the exported `developer_tools` build feature and matching verified playtest manifest must agree, and the version-specific persistence root must pass retail inspection before build authorization is granted. Ordinary release exports remain unmarked and reject developer mutation. Even a marked playtest requires the title-issued isolated solo Developer Garden or its temporary arena; ordinary multiplayer has no developer action privileges.
PracticeEncounter validates formations through the existing battlefield and registers combatants with EncounterRoster. Elite and boss scenarios use the single floor-leader slot and the ordinary boss preparation and phase owners. The Workbench reads a detached active ER01 report. Restoring practice health and replenishing a training target do not create healing credit. Ability bypass changes public ability deadlines only. Each reset destroys the temporary world and its effect owners.

BuildPlan starts with a detached class profile and empty card and graft ledgers. It validates connected class choices, class-local weapon indices and all three plant slots. The same grade calculation creates card offers for the checkpoint and the workbench. Each requested copy passes the ordinary eligibility and purchase planners. A temporary trophy budget pays for a graft only inside its detached plan. Replay defers unmet dependencies while other choices can still become legal. The editor previews unresolved copies before accepting a rebuilt loadout. Application replaces the practice world, including actor life, attack queues, affinity effects and statistics.

From Settings, open the Developer Garden, then choose Enter Testing Arena. F4 is rebindable. Controller Start opens or closes the workbench. Use Enemies to replace the current encounter, Build and Upgrades to stage a new loadout, and Statistics to inspect a round. Return to Developer Garden resumes the parked character. The arena runs solo. Optional neural training assets have no role in this session.

### Contact presentation and VFX Laboratory

The ordinary host publishes positive applied HP contacts and confirmed Brittle ruptures through the existing combat event stream. The renderer deduplicates events by floor and event ID. `combat_effects.gd` recognizes a weapon contact by its existing weapon form. It uses the recorded origin and target to orient the burst. A confirmed `frost_rupture` starts a separate, target-local effect. The associated HP receipt carries an optional `damage_cue` so its damage number can appear without a second flash at the same position. The ordinary Shatterwave, healing, incoming damage and block presentations retain their own paths. No particle data enters the combat snapshot.

`contact_particles.gd` shares one ArrayMesh per family and legal fragment count. Each active burst owns a fragment mesh instance and a small shader material holding its captured settings, event seed and elapsed time. VFX05A adds a child mist batch to crystalline ruptures. The compatibility shader in `assets/shaders/contact_fragments.gdshader` evaluates trajectories and facet color. Existing visibility, expiry, floor cleanup and round teardown own the live instances. Nearby rupture labels share a 200 ms window within 6.5 m of a displayed label. HP numbers and statistics retain individual receipts.

The Workbench Visuals tab hosts `developer_arena/contact_lab.gd`. Its private viewport contains a fixed camera, light and neutral reference targets. The same contact renderer plays against those targets without a combat world or event publisher. Production and experimental comparisons use matching seeds, positions, orientation and playback time. Hidden panels stop and clear their previews. The renderer captures experimental values on emission. DEV03's existing session allowlist and version-1 preset store own the overrides. Contact fingerprints cover the geometry source and shader. Existing presets continue through the same review and conflict handling. See [Wilds combat readability](COMBAT_READABILITY.md) for controls and values.

Ordinary combat uses `weapon_motion_trail.gd` after the stage samples the reviewed wrist and prop transforms. Each held prop owns its bounded world-space history and reuses one drawing surface. `weapon_delivery_visuals.gd` draws the authoritative flight segment, release accent, foot scuffs and inside-edge danger ticks. The outgoing hit owner can attach the swept intersection to a confirmed impact receipt. The existing target position and applied HP continue to drive labels and flashes. Dodge receipts follow the accepted movement and share floor-scoped event deduplication. Clients construct all geometry locally.

The Workbench's reviewed-sequence exhibit uses the traveller rig, weapon factory, motion sampler and projectile renderer in the private viewport. Tuning stays on the authorized scene instance. The [weapon review and controls](COMBAT_READABILITY.md) include the fixed presentation budgets.

### Fire, Frost and Thorn presentation

The field snapshot supplies Ember Wake’s terrain-clipped capsule and the remaining lifetime. Its baseline is 3.2 seconds, with a 2.15 m half-length and 0.68 m radius. Each snapshot reuses the field’s scorched roots, flame tongues, drifting embers and smoke. The cast receipt supplies the caption. The status stream places a smaller ignition and smolder on each Burning target. Burning and Bleed HP receipts retain their damage numbers. Flashover lifts a short tuft of flame at the source and Wildfire sheds embers. Each keeps its separately confirmed area cue.

Rime Bloom opens inside its 2.25 m circle. Its second receipt closes the same arrangement of crystal petals 625 ms later. The opening applies two Brittle stacks on contact and the ordinary closing applies one. Body crystals follow the displayed stack count. Each confirmed rupture owns the existing VFX02 target-local splinters and a soft frost wisp. Shatterwave retains its own area event. Detailed captions restore the closing label.

Thorn Core fields draw tapering brambles along the authoritative capsule. The ordinary Core uses a 2.25 m half-length, 0.65 m radius and seven-second lifetime. Form and Catalyst growth use the circular shapes actually admitted by combat, including the ward variant. Their new shoots rise at field admission. Burgundy sap follows the Bleed status until its retirement. A confirmed Hemorrhagic Rupture throws a brief spray of sap at its source while its area cue shows the reach of the payoff.

`affinity_glyphs.gd` builds one shared mesh per silhouette. Each field, status or phase has a local material clock. A project-authored four-mask atlas and the Compatibility shader provide the textured layers. The VFX Laboratory uses the same meshes and shaders for stepped and repeated playback. New artistic sliders use the existing developer tuning and preset validation. The mesh instances own no collision, status, damage or rewards. The field snapshot’s phase also allows a guest entering mid-cast to see mature growth.

[Normal-camera visual comparison](vfx05a-affinity-comparison.jpg) · [Moving comparison with full camera and detail](vfx05a-affinity-motion.webp)

### Water, Wood and Vital presentation

`shelter_visuals.gd` owns the cached silhouettes and six local artistic controls. Live combat and the Visuals tab use the same Compatibility shader and the Garden droplet and pollen atlas. Each visible batch has a local material clock. The wave sheet, bark grain and curved petals are project-authored. The droplets and pollen reuse the project-authored VFX03 atlas. The preset fingerprint includes the mesh builder, shader and atlas.

Dewfall uses the clipped start and end of its 550 ms sweep. Each admitted Tidal Return contributor has a distinct 450 ms outgoing phase and a 450 ms return phase. The phase receipt carries its actual width and elapsed offset. Monsoon uses its own expanding and contracting ring receipts. A moisture mark follows the enemy’s published Drenched count. An intercepted return produces restorative droplets only on a recipient with positive applied healing.

Ironbark plates follow the recipient’s current Bark status. Their opacity varies smoothly with remaining HP of protection. An admitted absorption emits one short chip cue at the recipient. Expiry retires the plates quietly. Splinterburst draws from the established expenditure and radial damage event. A directional Form accent marks the primary impact. Secondary splinters join the actual accepted contact endpoints, including penetrations.

Vital Bloom opens around its actual cast center. Low leaves follow an active Vitality recipient. The regeneration owner emits a recipient-local cue after HP increases, summing any catch-up ticks into one displayed amount per update. Existing per-tick provider attribution remains with expedition statistics. Increasing maximum health and spending bonus HP produce no restorative cue. None of these meshes participate in targeting, status admission or settlement.

### Storm, Spirit and Void presentation

`storm_affinity.gd::discharge` projects the accepted main, fork and return edges with the two positions already chosen by combat. The damage receipt retains its amount and carries a local flash cue. `spirit_affinity.gd::hints` projects each current preparation with its owner, activation identity, departure position and remaining time. Consuming an echo withdraws only that echo. The accepted replica carries the weapon clip, reach, direction and attack duration. A supported shadow cast uses the existing Wraithcast receipt. Historical suspended preparations with only a deadline appear already formed and expire at that deadline.

`core_combat.gd::hints` and `void_affinity.gd::hints` supply the real field center, radius, age and remaining time. The host identifies extra Singularity fields and emits separate collapse and target-local Rift Shear receipts. A lingering Well can keep gathering after its initial collapse. Clients reconcile field and preparation hints by identity on the existing combat projection. Reliable receipts retain their normal serial deduplication.

`astral_visuals.gd` owns the cached strip meshes and six artistic settings. The two project-authored shaders draw the local currents and the translucent traveller skin. The manifestation uses the displayed owner's rig and equipment with private pose state. Its weapon attaches straight to that model. The Workbench and live stage share this assembly and the reviewed weapon animation paths. Each scene owns its projection nodes. Expiry, hint withdrawal and round replacement release them. The Workbench comparison uses the existing tuning allowlist, per-property source fingerprints, local presets and presentation clock.

### Living Garden presentation

The five authored Heart meshes retain their bark, fissure pigment, development scale and reclamation light. An additional material pass breathes only over emissive fissure surfaces. Structure and frontier roots carry distance from the Heart in their second UV channel. A branch inherits the distance at its actual attachment point, including forks. The bark shader uses this distance to move a broken band of color through the existing grain. Its ordinary crest travels at 2.1 m/s with a 0.72 m half-width. The Heart and roots share a slightly irregular 0.18 Hz rhythm at their production settings.

`garden_infrastructure_view.gd` derives spray, pollen and shore batches from placed poses. Stored stations contribute no geometry. Water effects also require the resolved supply flag. Inlet rings converge at the computed shore tip. Sprinkler droplets follow small ballistic arcs and short broken threads at the spouts. Pollination Blooms carry an elevated, wind-bent flower and a few warm motes. Their walking outline retains the pre-VFX03 footprint. The editable, project-authored `assets/terrain_m02/garden_motes.svg` contains four alpha masks. The Compatibility renderer samples these masks on batched geometry. Live motion uses shader time. Disabling Garden ambient motion quiets the fissure, roots and flower and hides the water and pollen batches for that viewer. Irrigation and cultivation continue on their ordinary clocks.

In F4 → Tuning → Visuals, choose a Garden effect. The private viewport reuses the production materials, station mesh recipes and root planner. Choose a Heart stage or toggle water supply, play, pause, step, and switch between Production baseline and Experimental at the same elapsed time. The live Developer Garden also reflects accepted visual edits. The artistic controls and defaults live in `assets/terrain_m02/garden_ambient.gd`. Existing preset storage handles color, timing, density and shape controls. Decorative throw and drift distances have no cultivation consumers. The [normal-camera comparisons](vfx03-garden-comparison.jpg) and [controlled playback of all five effects](vfx03-garden-motion.gif) show the initial treatment.

VFX03 captured 6.5 seconds per layout and cap at 1100 × 720 on an RTX 5080, using Godot 4.7.2 GL Compatibility with VSync disabled. The developed scene had one supplied sprinkler and one Bloom. The larger layouts used three and five of each, respectively, on ten purchased frontiers. At sixty beds the three cosmetic batches submitted 408 quads in total, including inactive droplet slots and short strips. The renderer culls each batch as a unit. Distant emitters inside a visible batch still incur vertex work.

| Rendered Garden | 120 FPS cap, median / p95 | Uncapped, median / p95 | Last-frame draws |
|---|---|---|---|
| Starting | 8.30 / 8.47 ms | 2.11 / 2.25 ms | 378 |
| Developed | 8.25 / 8.46 ms | 2.66 / 2.82 ms | 458 |
| 30 beds | 8.28 / 8.46 ms | 2.82 / 3.05 ms | 458 |
| 60 beds | 8.33 / 8.50 ms | 3.07 / 3.23 ms | 524 |

Before the change, the starting and developed scenes measured 8.47 and 8.46 ms capped p95 and 2.29 and 2.82 ms uncapped p95. VFX01 measured 8.47 and 8.48 ms capped p95 in those Gardens. The VFX02 contact benchmarks exercised combat. Whole-process frame times include the game and interface. Individual frames reached 31 ms. These captures cover one local renderer and leave CPU/GPU attribution, lower-end devices and multiplayer rendering performance unmeasured.

### Developer Workbench tuning (DEV03)

Open the Developer Workbench with F4 or controller Start in the Developer Garden, its Wilds expedition, or the Testing Arena. The same panel provides Gameplay, Garden, World, Visuals and Presets under Tuning. Testing retains the arena entry between expeditions. The Workbench displays the source baseline, proposed numeric input, applied setting and application timing. A gameplay edit is staged until Apply. Visual edits take effect as they are entered. Reset restores the selected property, category or full set for future operations.

The title-issued Garden owns one in-memory `developer_arena/tuning.gd` instance. Its allowlist names each value, bounds, units, source and application boundary. The parked Garden lends that instance to its private solo arena, including across round and build replacement. Overrides are applied to the active scene and synchronized on return. Ordinary worlds have no tuning owner. Mutation and file operations check the authorized build, exact active world, Garden lease, solo transport and developer-session setting. Disabling the setting clears overrides. Closing the developer session discards them. Developer Save & Quit refuses pending or applied gameplay differences with instructions to save a preset and reset. Visual-only settings can be discarded safely on suspension. The existing resume path retains accepted attacks, stored floor outcomes and collection claims.

| Property | Production owner | Experimental range and timing |
|---|---|---|
| Combat impact emission | `combat_effects.gd`, `IMPACT_EMISSION` (0.65) | 0–4×. The next hit gets a fresh material. The Compatibility renderer also scales the unshaded color around the production value. Visuals can replay either equipped class ability with a baseline or tuned impact. Replay uses only the renderer and holds at most two transient visuals. |
| Scene sun energy | `stage.gd::apply_scene_sun` and the current biome climate | 0–4×, immediate. The scene file starts at 1.2, camera focus requests 1.05 in the Garden or 1.35 in the Wilds, and terrain presentation can apply its climate afterward. Reset restores the latest production lighting request on the scene's own Sun. |
| Briar Spear base reach | Warden weapon `0_1` in `class_catalog.gd` (3.1 m) | 0.5–8 m. The basic attack port adjusts the detached attack packet before admission, retaining the build's reach bonuses. The telegraph, hit checks and queued strike share the captured geometry. |
| Living Sprinkler base radius | `garden_structure_catalog.gd`, sprinkler radius (3.5 m) | 0.5–10 m. Apply credits the pending growth interval at its old rate, invalidates the irrigation owner's layout cache and recalculates coverage. The inspector and placement preview query that owner. Researched 5 m coverage still takes precedence. |
| Ordinary Wild Flora appearance | `wild_flora_generation.gd::SPAWN_CHANCE` (40%) | 0–100%. The next eligible floor uses the existing isolated flora stream and placement rules. Introductory guarantees remain. If Returning Root is researched, experimental generation reserves the root site that the production flora roll would have allowed. |

`developer_arena/tuning_presets.gd` stores version-1 JSON in `<Developer Garden storage>/tuning/`. Files contain only differences, with stable property IDs, units, types, original and proposed values, source references, Git HEAD when available and per-source SHA-256 fingerprints. Scene lighting includes its biome source. A debug template with stripped source records a distinctly tagged runtime fingerprint. Compare reports changed values and source revisions. A changed source requires review even at the same Git commit. Incompatible formats, unknown IDs, duplicate entries and invalid values reject the whole load. Confirming a reviewed preset replaces the session configuration. Named presets survive restart and require explicit loading.

Presets can be saved, duplicated, renamed or deleted after confirmation. Export current changes or a selected preset to create `changes.md` and `changes.json` in a fresh developer `tuning/exports/` folder. The Workbench displays that folder. Both formats identify differences from today's baseline and any unresolved conflicts for review. Applying an approved proposal to production is a separate source change.

### Reproducible developer scenarios (DEV04A)

The Testing Arena's Scenarios tab captures an applied build and encounter setup. Its version-1 JSON lives in the Developer Garden's separate `scenarios/` directory. The developer file helper provides scoped naming, bounded reads and temporary-file publication for scenarios and tuning presets. Each uses its own namespace and authorization. Scenarios carry stable class, weapon, material, progression, upgrade and enemy IDs, the P1 participant slot, ordered acquisitions with rarity, practice controls, encounter depth, terrain seed, formation and an optional preset name. Repeated acquisitions encode actual stacks. The record includes its originating Git commit when available and a fingerprint of the gameplay definitions. Built-in scenarios can be copied. Their definitions stay in source.

`developer_arena/scenarios.gd` resolves the record against current catalogs and runs the existing build planner and formation checks before accepting a load. Unknown content and rejected dependencies produce an inspection error. Changed source requires review. The practice session replaces the temporary world, recreates terrain with the ordinary battlefield generator and replays validated acquisitions. The new round starts with fresh statistics, actions, enemies and effects. Workbench settings and explicitly selected tuning survive replay. Suggested presets require a separate load. A fixed seed reproduces the starting field and formation. Inputs, combat rolls and enemy decisions can vary.

Seed zero selects the practice clearing. Positive seeds select generated terrain and bosses retain their dedicated setup. Depth 1–600 is a testing control. The terrain laboratory remains a separate visual preview. Scenario descriptions can reference a setup in the parked Developer Garden. Reproducing a developed Garden on another machine would require a validated placement manifest or dedicated authored layout fixture.

**Cooperative practice boundary (DEV04B assessment).** `session_world.gd::register` requires write access to the host Garden before admitting an ordinary guest. Guest admission and departure also own character profile and settlement operations. Practice has memory-only profiles and denies writes. A disposable probe confirmed that ordinary registration refuses a temporary practice participant at this boundary.

`developer_arena/session.gd::_replace` destroys the previous combat world on each clean replay. `game.gd::_exit_tree` calls `session_world.offline`, which closes the transport and clears its participant bindings. `world_replication_source.gd::state_key` uses route, floor and seed. Replaying seed 117 at encounter 2 reproduced the same key. The ordinary guest receiver accepted a delayed frame numbered 23 from the previous round and then dropped frame 1 of the new round when those frames were presented to the fresh receiver. The solo arena has no network delivery path.

Cooperative practice needs an authenticated practice session that retains participant slots and the connection across world replacement, admits and releases temporary characters independently of save settlement, and identifies which round owns replicated input, snapshots and effects. Ordinary Garden networking and developer practice currently retain their separate permissions.

### Class and advanced combat presentation (VFX06)

Native casts and focus releases use the existing combat event stream. Rootshield supplies its captured facing via the existing yaw field and Guiding Bell supplies a valid width for the resolved pull. The stage selects class artwork by event kind and shape. Combat code still owns the cast, affected targets, cooldown and pose. Avalanche publishes a presentation link inside accepted secondary stack resolution. Harvest publishes an actor-local cue only after positive bonus HP expenditure. The native graft owner marks its three delayed contact cues with their source. These additions use existing event fields and skip the damage, healing and mitigation accounting paths.

The stage owns the lifetimes of class strokes and build payoffs. Only active field and preparation hints participate in the presentation overlap calculation. Layout changes update co-located preparation captions and core opacity. Expired hints, round resets and actor replacement dispose of their presentation children. The solo laboratory uses the same stroke mesh, materials and normalized playback functions in a private viewport. Class, Unique and adaptation scenarios reconstruct builds through the existing eligibility-aware scenario loader.

## Portable character inventory (INV01)

The inventory owner remains `InventoryModel` and the existing Garden reserve. `inventory_custody.gd` defines the versioned handoff record; `inventory_custody_world.gd` integrates it with the already-authorized Garden/profile serializers. This is enabled for normal guarded title sessions. Legacy source/debug worlds and isolated Developer Garden/practice retain their existing paths and may not open converted inventory saves via the legacy route. The production migration cutover flag is unchanged.

* **Home:** `Character.inventory_custody` holds the canonical spendable payload. Known seeds and parts are keyed by stable catalog IDs; concealed seed order, Ancient lineage counts, extra items and seed-selection history are retained. A local checkout and check-in use the existing paired Garden/profile transaction.
* **Away:** a profile records destination Garden origin, endpoint, monotonically increasing sequence, random token and outbound digest. The frozen outbound payload is for retries only. The Garden owns the spendable reserve under the persistent character identity. Matching retries rebind to the existing inventory, not the original outbound quantities. A different Garden or unverified local session cannot admit the locked character.
* **Return:** the host settles gameplay first, waits for confirmed profile delivery and commits a return receipt while clearing the spendable reserve. The peer is no longer ready for gameplay before publication. The guest commits the matching returned payload to its own profile, then acknowledges; the Garden keeps only the terminal sequence/token tombstone when the acknowledgment arrives. A lost acknowledgment cannot reopen inventory; a later strictly newer checkout supersedes the old receipt. An interrupted guest reconnects to the same Garden for a committed return, without replaying the expedition settlement.

The existing guard and transaction owners preserve verified abort, confirmed commit, committed cleanup-pending and uncertainty. A crash before a conclusive remote transfer can leave the character locked; do not unlock on timeout, address reuse or copied stock. Recover the originating Garden with its matching durable record. If that Garden is permanently lost or unavailable the offline architecture cannot safely infer its final inventory. Arbitrarily edited/cloned character saves are not an authenticated account system. Suspended or parked expedition participants retain custody until they resume or settle; the established retention policy for unbanked runtime loot is unchanged.

Legacy keyed guest reserves are combined atomically on first checkout in that Garden. The historical unkeyed host reserve requires explicit assignment to a selected character with reviewed Garden/profile paths and durable digests; the same commit clears the old namespace and records that assignment. A pre-Archive save may receive a provisional random origin on inspection, so confirmation binds the actual selected files rather than that transient origin. The first conversion uses the existing migration-backup facility. Shared resources are excluded from every handoff. Locked characters cannot be edited/deleted and Gardens with outstanding custody cannot be removed or replaced.

UI01 source selection, shared withdrawals, mixed crafting, reservation rules and individual Returning Root allowances are unchanged. Arrival with a known seed does not award Knowledge or make the seed mysterious. Planting an identified seed for the first time in that Garden uses its existing one-time discovery ledger; active checked-out and shared stock do not invent discoveries during a modern save load. Legitimate expedition banking retains its existing discovery behavior.
